knowledge, and power was that which is used for good or bad as the user sees fit.
The next morning, they had to get more food, for the journey, as well as the extra
provisions for Nia. Andy had not told her about the sword, though he would very soon, after
they were on the move. She understood what they could be facing, though she did not anticipate
a sword, perhaps only hiding and using wit to escape. Nia was used to the walking by now, but it
would still be hard to do so every day.
Other items they had brought were a pot for cooking, as well as other utensils, tools for
starting a fire, in case there was a cold night or bad weather. They also brought some extra
blankets in case they needed it for themselves or for other material use.
They were to depart in an hour and Andy had everything they needed, it was midday, and
they were ready to leave, though they wanted one last proper meal from the inn before they left.
When the meal was finished, and they were ready for their departure, they went out the north
gate that was closest to the river.
From there, they would walk to the river, which might take three days, then follow the
river into the center of the land, then down with the other river into the forest south. When they
reached the forest, they did not know what to do then, which was going to be about three weeks
of travel, especially in the forest. Whatever they did though, they were to reach the forest and be
able to rest there, without much worry, or so they thought.
As they walked, they talked, they talked about that which was of no concern to them, at
least deep concern, and they enjoyed it. The talking took the stress off their legs as they walked
and they had not to worry about their legs. Andy was not going to share what he had discovered
about Liam's revelation to his mind, because he did not want Nis to be guilty or think
something was her fault. Liam could have chosen to stop that which he was doing at any time,
he was capable. But what bothered him most about the betrayal was the fact that he had made
the connections, and then severed them in a moment's notice. However he was involved in the
lightning striking, or the strange storm, he was guilty, and he would get what was coming for
him.
Andy could tell that Nia was a little tired as they walked, probably from the previous
walking, and perhaps the strain on her mind about the recent events. He also was thinking about
the things that had happened. When a pause split their conversation, Andy knew they were both
thinking about the events. Andy, though, was thinking about what Eradris was doing. He
wondered if he should share his presence with Nia. He decided to ask.
Eradris? He thought as he reached for the bird in all directions.
He did not have to search far, but he did have to search high, as he felt the bird and his
wise mind. What do you need?
I want to know if I should share your presence with my sister, Nia. Do you have an opinion? She
can also talk to you if needed, it would be useful in a risky situation.
I will let you choose. Well yes, do it.
Thank you.
As he returned to himself, he looked at Nia, “I have something to tell you.”
“Yes?” She asked, with her normal smile.
“Well, I figured out what that voice was, and it is a falcon. He can talk better than the
rest of the animals and he can also talk to you if you like. He is very wise.”
“Where is he?” she asked, already looking to the sky.
“Yes, reach above us, he is watching over us, just in case he needs to warn us.”
He could tell she was reaching, but was not far enough to not be able to walk. When she
smiled, he knew that she had found him. He did not know what she was saying to Eradris, but, if
past experience was anything to judge by, she was thanking him and introducing herself to him.
Eradris would not lie to her, he would never lie, especially when trust was being built. To tear
down, trust before it was built was not a good thing. Trust was not solid or gained over a matter
of minutes, it had to be demonstrated. True trust is to trust that your friends will try to help you.
Andy had had much time to think about different concepts like that, he had often made small
pieces of poetry in his head, and one returned to him now. Like the wind on the grass, he recited
it in his mind perfectly.
In my true trust
We will not rust
Without the light
Trust my sight
To guide you in the night
It was not the best of words, but it was a wonderful little verse. The verse he had come
up with a few years ago. He had not remembered for quite a while, many he had forgotten. He
then realized that you do not remember anything, yet you retain the information.
As they were traveling on the grass, for the terrain was very grassy, they were able to get
about five miles in about three hours. They had left one hour after noon. The time was now four
hours past noon. They thought about eating, for they had not had lunch, but they retained their
desire until it was time for the small dinner they were to have. They needed to also keep an eye
out for anything that was edible along the way that they could eat to save themselves from
expending the food they already had.
When the time came to eat the evening meal of potatoes that were left over from Andy’s
trip to the town. He knew he was going to have to save them, if he wanted to enjoy them
throughout the trip. It was going to be a longer trip to get to the forest than on foot and would
be harder on their feet. They did not say much as they were eating except for the preparation of
the meal that they had their own preferences about.
The potatoes were already baked and extra as well as filling as they always were, but it
felt better after walking for hours. Hours of the energy only draining from your limbs was very
exhausting. Food was something very essential to their plan. He did not want to do that which
would be almost sure to hurt them. He knew many of the plants that were edible and without a
poison so he could eat and collect them for Nia too. Nia wanted to learn what plants were okay
to eat so they could both search for them, or in case one of them missed something, the other
might catch the slip.
That night, they did not have enough wood to start a fire so they went to sleep without
the warmth of the fire to aid them. Though a fire would be nice, they knew it was impossible.
They considered packing wood with them, but that would put too much strain on them, and
they wouldn’t be able to adapt quickly enough to the weight. If they were to pack wood it would
have to be over time that they accustomed themselves to the weight.
As they slept and the night filled by, Andy had a strange dream. The dream was in his
perspective with his narration.
I walked out of the empty castle and saw many piles of black, all in lines. Some sort of pattern
this precise was not to be formed by nature or by any means nature could muster. Some powerful thing
had turned the thousands of rows into something so precise that nothing could be made out of their
composition. I walked to the first line and saw the deathly color of blackish red on a small piece of metal.
My perspective was blurred and I could not see what was on the piece, but it must have been something
important.
Then as I looked farther out into the country my sight replicated what I had seen in front of me.
Piles of black dust as far as the eye could see, and they rolled with the hills and moved with the terrain.
What is this?
As the dream faded back into regular sleep, Andy loathed to leave it though it disturbed
him. He was free in his dreams, floating around without any responsibility.
In the morning, he was well rested and he was also content despite the dream.
The next day of travel, they were entering more rugged land and not quite so lush. The
land was rough and covered with rocks, though the scenery was still quite beautiful. The rocks
were like a natural puzzle spreading its pieces throughout that area. The rocks jutted out of the
ground everywhere like sharp knives protruding out of the earth. It was quite hard to navigate
and there was no plant life suitable for eating. When Andy and Nia entered the rock forest, the
rocks rounded on the base to make an egg shaped ball. The rock marbles were daunting and
frightening when they stood before them, and Andy imagined running through the valley and
chasing them.
The rocks were not fun to traverse, but they certainly took strange shapes. The shapes
were like animals, people and even houses too, as well as the egg shaped ones. The strange rocks
covered the land and gave an eerie feeling to the landscape. They felt watched.
As they continued, the way they had seen before they had entered was fading in Andy’s
mind though he tried hard to keep it there. The feeling died down after a while but the
forgetfulness remained, and increased. The terrain seemed to be moving, though if it was, Andy
would be slipping. He squinted trying to keep his vision clear. He had not seen anything magical
or felt anything even, they were just uneasy in their sight and it started to give them a headache.
Then all at once the weariness, forgetfulness, watching feeling ceased. In front of them
stood a tall rock, higher than the others, blocking the sun's warm rays. It felt as if the mountain
was the thing that had been watching them. It could very well be the reason.
A sound rumbled up behind them and they both turned to see what it was, the sound
continued as they looked, but all they could see was the same rocks that had been there before.
They looked at each other before looking back up to gaze at the mountainous protrusion. Andy
felt weak before it. Then he looked at Nia as the sound’s volume increased. They looked
everywhere this time, but they only saw the same grey rock surrounding them.
In his mind, Andy said, We should not linger. He included Nia in the thought and they
moved out of the shadow of the mountain. The mountain left its shadow where it was though,
when they stepped into the sun light ceased to filter through to them. The mountain had seemed
to shift a little.
Run. Nia said.
“I agree.”
“Let's get out of here, I don’t want the mountain to follow us, a mountain would be a
formidable enemy.” She said out loud.
Without another word, they started to run, then the rumbling shook the ground too, and
the mountain seemed to follow them as they ran and the rumbling only grew more immense.
Suddenly, the rocks that were shaped like balls on the bottom burst out of their
foundation and sprouted legs out of the ground. They saw the light pour over them again and

and saw the mountain shuddering and shaking with the movement stone. The top grumbled,
then cracked with a loud boom. Dust rained down and rubble rained on them.
Out of the top was a hand, made out of stone with bony joints. The sides rumbled and
cracked, revealing the rest of the forearm and fell on the floor. As soon as it was on the ground,
it used its fingers to pull itself toward them. Not only that, but the rest of the creatures that
sprouted out of the rocks like colossal crabs were stretching their old, long legs, very slowly, and
very quietly. They continued to run.
The giant crabs with their massive back peaks. The crabs were not out to hurt them, but
to hinder them. Their legs and bodies started to form walls to keep them from passing while the
hand pulled itself through the maze like rocks with ease. They however, were stopped in their
tracks not knowing where they were to go. The crabs continued to form a wall around them and

The walls solidified again and the cliffs were too sheer to climb. They had to find their
way out of the maze before they couldn’t. They ran again, motivated to escape days of chase
starvation. They had to escape the rocks.
They ran through the labyrinth, and the voice came into their heads, the voice of Eradris.
He fed his view from above, though it remained confusing because of the height of the rocks.
They turned left, then right, then right again, and then again. They continued forward and they

The rose and connected slowly but dangerously.

Andy and Nia ran and when they risked to stop and look back, the monster had gained

Follow the final light

They knew they would have to get into the forest, or else something much worse would
happen. They stood and ran promptly into the forest so as not to be caught by the giant. Andy
knew that they were going to make it, but he was also curious what the giant would do when
they got there. When they were safely inside the forest and under its canopy, they turned to see
what the giant would do next. It looked confused, as if it did not know why it was running
toward the forest. It looked directly at them, and yet, its eyes passed over them without a second
look. They were successful.
Andy was still dumbstruck by the amount of weight he had lifted and the appearance of
the roots through some magical phrase. He wondered where the strange new voice had come
from, he had absolutely no idea what it could have been. He told Nia as they stood up and looked
at the events that had transpired in front of them. The giant laid down itself and started to
merge with the ground again, right in the middle of the stony plain. Andy wondered if it was the
change in the region that it could not notice or if it simply could not see beyond its own region.
Andy was not going to linger in this area, they needed to find the river again. The river
had to be in the forest, behind them so they both agreed, finding the river would be their first
step.
5: Follow Me
As they followed the river, which was shallow in the farthest and not very far into it,
Andy and Nia talked, talking as they had talked before but with more thought and care with
their words. He knew they were going to reach their destination when they came across the
river's turning point. The river wound throughout the forest and the forest let the river flow, and
rather grew with it. They reached the river’s re-entrance into the forest very quickly and they
were not expecting the feeling of hesitance that stopped them.
The feeling was cautious but also urged them into the forest.
Eladris entered both of their minds, Go, you must not linger here, other things lurk where we
do not see. We can talk and speculate about the feeling later; now, we must plunge into the forest.
“He is right, we dare not hesitate.”
As they entered, the feeling disappeared and all the desires that came with it. Andy
thought the feeling strange, and he had too many of those feelings to just ignore them. He was
still trying to decipher the riddle poem that Eradris had given him. He could not know
everything at once, though he promised himself to come back to the thought. Could all of this be
interconnected? It probably was, but Andy was too distracted to think about it further.
The trees took weird shapes as they progressed deeper into the forest. They grew taller
too. Pines and dark solid oak trees were what the forest composed itself of. He had a thought
occur to him, Could the trees have a mind? How is it that they were able to stop that rock? He was
compelled to find the answer and he was sure at least a clue was hidden within the dark forest.
He also knew that if the clues were to be anywhere, and if trees did have minds, then they would
want to protect the knowledge.
The conversation stopped between them when they had entered the forest, but the forest
seemed to have air that pressed into every gap and seemed to divide Andy and Nia. They could
still contact the other, but it felt off, as if they were sitting in an uncomfortable position. They
did not want more things to worry about and to add to their plate of concerns, for it was
growing with every passing minute. The time itself seemed to be suppressed by the air as well as
the air suppressing itself. The air was hard to breathe and the gravity seemed to increase.
They got used to the feeling after a while and the separation did not seem to be as
prominent as they continued to move. Strange thoughts entered their mind though they did not
think them, and voices seemed to be heard amongst the trees, giggling and laughing that they
were thinking those things. It was almost as if they laughed because of a charade or a distraction
that had succeeded. But as soon as the voices were heard, they died in the air and they might not
have even heard them at all.
They examined the scenery and saw all of the strange shapes, fallen moss covered logs,
and many other things nature decorated. Nature gave them much to look at and wonder about it.
Though their surroundings continued to get darker, their hue was lighter, as if from a reflected
light. The light was a greenish color and did not have a very bright shine.
As they continued walking though, the color did grow in brightness. It was not a natural
color. Something about the color seemed to move as they looked at it. When the light was at the
height of its brightness, they saw a strange thing. Moving, it was, animal it was, but not like the
animals they had ever seen. What was it? It appeared as a snail, but it was green and about
twenty times bigger than the traditional snail. It had an antenna that curled at the top and small
dark eyes that held deep darkness. What made the glow though, was the green glowing swirls of
light following the shell's lines. It was very bright.
The snail seemed to notice their presence and its colors started to pulse which was very
engaging to their eyes.
Nia was wary of the snail and she did not seem to be affected by the snail's lull. Andy
however was interested, not entranced in the snail, but rather curious to see what it would do.
He reached out to it, but the snail made no move to get out of his way. Whatever this creature
was or where it came from, it might know the forest and probably be able to give useful clues on
where to go in the forest.
Andy withdrew as Nia grabbed his hand and pulled it away, “Try with your mind first.
We do not want another accident.”
He nodded and then thrust his mind at the snail. He did not feel anything, but then he
felt the throbbing of its mind, the throbbing matched the pulsing on its shell. When he touched
the creature's mind, it was like a floaty sensation, like everything was combined into one
motivation, but without much organization. Andy had never tried to touch a snail's mind before
and he was not sure he liked it now that he had. It was not driven by much conscious thought,
but by instinct or habit. He formed a question and projected it to the snail. The snail did not
reply except to stop its green pulse of light.
Who are you? What is your business in this forest?
We seek the great city here.
There are thousands of those here, one even right beneath your feet.
What about a city inhabited by people?
Ah, you will undoubtedly find it, you do not need my help. What do you want this for, to get to
the city? What is it you seek there?
I seek revenge and we both seek safety and knowledge about our changing world. If we are going
to live here with everyone, we might as well try to understand as much as we can about the different ways
of the world.
You will find what you desire, for a time, that is. Others also seek the knowledge here, and not
quite so humbly as you do. I will find you slipping around another time, for my words are not complete.
With that, the snail slowly moved away, the way that Andy and Nia had come. They
wondered what other sort of creatures lived under the dark canopy. Because the snail’s light
faded, it was now dark again. The darkness closed in around them, though they did not feel
weak, nor hungry. They were, however, not the same as they were when they entered the forest.
They did not feel right.
“Do you feel something?” Andy asked Nia.
“I feel light headed, but I do not know what it could be from. It almost feels like I haven’t
eaten for days.”
Oh! They had not eaten for days, that was it! He voiced his thought, “Yes, we need to eat, we
have not eaten in a while, and we have been running.”
Though it was hard to see in the dark, and they fumbled with the packages and bags that
they had, they still knew what to get and what it felt like. When they had quenched their hunger,
as well as thirst. They were wary as they ate though not to make noise, they did not want to draw
the unwanted attention that could be waiting for them. They had to be more careful to pay more
attention to their surroundings, and not to just think and walk but feel. They could reach around
them with their minds to feel another person's mind if they wanted to. If there were people
close, they would feel them, but if they discovered another strange being like the snail, they
wanted to be prepared.
They proceeded as aware and cautious as they could and took in the scenery, though
dark it may be. They dared not sleep in a strange place like this forest, but they may have no
choice. If they got tired enough…
Andy risked words in the dark strange forest, “If we were not able to feel our hunger, we
may not be able to feel our other… things, like sleeping. Even though it is probably dangerous,
we do need to sleep.”
Nia nodded but did not say anything as they quietly unpacked their blankets to sleep.
They folded them over the ground and slept side by side, just in case they were discovered by
some other-worldly creature again. As they laid down, their sleep came almost instantly, and
Andy’s suspicion had been confirmed by the results. He did not dream, but sank into his sleep
very quickly. He felt as content as anyone could be and he suddenly did not remember any of his
cares. He did not have any dreams, but the voices they had heard returned to his own memory.
They were not very far into the forest and already strange things had happened. They did
not know if they should go forward in the forest, now it seemed an idea that could turn you
around. They feared as they slept that if they were to go farther into the forest, they would get
lost and become more confused. More strange thoughts entered his mind. A thought of the snail
and the rock giant combined entered his mind, and it seemed amusing. He would have laughed,
but no noise was able to escape his mouth.
Many other strange creatures combined in his mind too and they also seemed amusing.
Sometimes the creatures would talk to him and they got mad when he did not talk back to them.
He thought he saw his brother in one of the creatures' backgrounds he had not yet come across,
though as he passed to the next thought, the previous one eluded his grasp, so he forgot what it
meant, or if it did mean anything.
Andy had many dreams that night, though as they progressed they transitioned from
funny, to frightening. One was that he saw the rock giant talking into the darkness which Andy
did not know why.
Andy's thoughts were renewed with greater clarity when he woke up, because he knew
that he was not still asleep. The dreams had dragged on and did not leave him feeling like he
wanted to go on, neither did he feel he was capable. That thought was the very reason he was
able to get up.
The light had returned, though everything was covered in a green tint. The world was
not as it usually was, but that also was the thing that was not strange about it. Andy had gotten
used to the world going somewhere or doing something he did not expect. The green gave a
different view on the forest and didn’t appear to be as gloomy as before. The shapes in the trees
also began to look pleasant, if not seemingly, fading from the trees as the forest dove inward.
Nia was already up, though Andy had not noticed. She returned from somewhere in the
forest and said, “Is the forest not beautiful? It is so very random; one night it is playing tricks on
our mind, while now it looks dazzling and wonderful.”
“Yes, I agree. I wonder what causes the changes.” Andy replied, even though he was still
thinking of the strange dreams and how he had lifted the boulder. He thought that something in
the forest was the cause for both. But why would it help them and then promptly confuse them.
It did not make sense.
“Did you have any strange dreams last night?” Andy asked Nia.
“I thought I was the only one, but it must have been something here in the forest.” She
said with a ponderous look on her face.
“Something tells me, that the tree roots that saved me, the voices, the dreams, all of the
strange occurrences are coming from someone or something in the forest. I do not like that, if it
is the same thing, it keeps confusing us.”
“I thought about that too.”
Andy’s only reply was, “Hmmm,” whatever it was it had to be related, and it had to be
important. What could it be that had such power? Andy could not even guess. He knew little of
the magical world, except for his own ability.
They did not speak anymore, but they ate, despite the feeling not to, as well as packing
up their provisions. It did not take long for them to do those things.
They were able to travel a fair distance that day, and the trees did lose their odd shapes,
though they did not lose their tremendous height. He knew that the trees would not lose their
height, but he did know for a fact that there was something controlling the strange events in the
forest. He was grateful that he was able to travel the distance that he had. They needed the
progress, and he was enjoying the forest’s beautiful scenery. He was happier than he had been
the whole journey and content too. He was happy about that which he had and did not wish for
more. He thought he sensed himself with a hint of humility.
As they were traveling that day, they heard the voices again, but they were sure they
heard them out loud this time. They were floaty and they were singing now. Their voices held a
strange sadness to them, but they were also singing very loud so you could hear all around. Then
the voice harmonized with others all around them and started getting louder, and closer. The
voice was not a pitch any human could match with his or her voice. It had a very high pitched
voice and a strange tune.
He knew that it was most likely not a good idea to linger, and his suspicions were only
confirmed as Eradris spoke to him, You must move!
“Nia, let’s run.” Andy said with a smile on his face.
She smiled, and they took off. They ran with all speed, but still the voice grew louder.
They only hoped to find something in the forest or get a moderate distance before whatever was
there found them. They kept running. All they could do was run. And they ran like the wind over
the stormy sea. Fast they ran, and faster the voices approached as they did. It was only after they
had been running for ten minutes that they realized how far away the voices had been compared
to where they sounded like they were.
They ran for another five minutes and the voices were at a peak, the pitch too high to
think comprehensible. They had to stop, so they came into a clearing in the woods to stop. They
covered their ears but the sound still penetrated their hands.
Andy thought his ears would burst, but right as the thought entered his mind, the
singing stopped abruptly, though it left his ears ringing. When he was able to see again and
comprehend what he was seeing, Andy realized that he had closed his eyes and clenched his
teeth. He saw thousands of little green ghostlike spirits. The spirits stared at the both of them,
giggling and pointing at them. They watched in return all the while their ears stung.
As the spirits did not talk directly, Andy decided he would try to engage them in a
conversation. “What do you want with us? Why do you crowd us?” Andy asked. “Could you tell
us what you are?”
The spirits seemed to be dumbfounded that they had spoken and they seemed afraid and
in a bustle too. The spirits then looked at them in unison and they then rushed forward to
examine them, all of them seemed to want a turn, and they did not wait for another to get out of
the way but squished right into the circle that was quickly forming around them. They all looked
the same except some that were different colors but not many, the ones that were different
colors though were only about one every three hundred and were red.
They then all seemed to be content and back away. And in the same manner of
symmetrical and perfect harmony, they said, “Come. We will guide you to the great city. For that
is what you seek. Is it not?”
Cautiously, Andy replied, with approval from Nia, “Yes, we seek the city, do you plan to
lead us there, or do you want us to go on our own?”
One removed itself from the others and said, “Follow.”
All of them helped and guided them into the front of a single file line. Only four of the
spirits were ahead of Andy and Nia in the line, and they were allowed to walk side by side, as
Nia and Andy were too. Their spirits started to march all in unison though they didn’t have legs.
They made the marching motion with their bodies by bobbing up and down. And they started to
sing as they walked along.
Follow the big and wonderful star,
Travel on to your great home afar.
Shine with green light
And glide through the night
Then see the star above your sight
Show your bright
And joyful light
And never give
Without a fight.
Many more verses followed and those were only the first out of nine hundred they had
said. Too many, Andy thought, but he knew that it could be because they had so much time to
use and they may have even sung for longer than they had previously heard. Much of their song
was composed of facts about the world and what you were to do with the knowledge gained
from the world outside the forest, of which they had a significant amount stored in their city.
Though it was a long journey, the spirits were courteous and even brought Nia and Andy
a snack every once an hour. The food was strangely similar to the traditional food they had in
their village, though it tasted differently and filled them quicker than normal. A few beverages
they brought as well, they were red and tasted tart as cranberries do when you eat them. The
drinks, also, rejuvenated them and gave them enough strength for a day.
They were approaching night, when the constant straight path they had taken, led them
into darkness. The spirits glowed with a dim light, but were still bright enough for them to see
their footing. The forest darkened though and they could not see too far in any direction. The
canopy blocked any moonlight from entering through. As they walked through there was light
that appeared up ahead. It was a warm and welcoming light, and they did not, for once in the
strange forest, they did not feel strange, and all of their joyful thoughts that they could
comprehend as well as feel hope in enjoying the feelings.
They were happy to see that there might be something other than trees in the forest that
seemed to go on forever.
6: Oakenash City
They continued to walk through the forest, and a path seemed to open before them, and
the path on either side lit up as well when the forest seemed to lean in on them. A clear dirt path
formed as they continued, that they were not able to see before. The light increased in
brightness as well as the canopy, even though they had not been walking for very long in the
night time. It was too early for the sun to be up and they knew the moonlight would not
penetrate the thick canopy at all. Something else had to have pierced the canopy, something
under the cover of the forest.
Even as they walked, the darkness faded out of the forest as the light came closer. The
light looked more organized and constructed as they kept treading. The wood became lighter
too, not only more seeable but the oak seemed to brighten in its base color. Everything was
brighter, as if it grew better under the light than it did under the regular sunlight. The trees
were the tallest here and still getting taller, Andy wondered just how tall one could get.
The light was brighter as they got close to the opening where it came through. The light
was warm, welcoming, and gave them joy in their heart, it was not a rude dampened light.
And as they emerged into the opening, they saw a great oak tree, covered with lanterns
and stretching high above and over the other trees. In the tree were thousands of openings and
areas for use. The tree stood thousands of feet in the sky giving light over the forest canopy.
Other trees grew upon this tree, though they were not all the same kind of tree. As they grew
out, most of them were ash trees that had rooted themselves in the massive branches or roots.
There were also a few spruce and birch trees growing on the great oak.
“This great oak is who we are. This great tree is the mother of all other trees! Behold it,
and be grateful for it.”
They were certainly humble against the great branches of the tree, that even they
stretched fifty feet wide at the smallest, which gave them a chill running down their spine. The
tree was overall daunting with its tremendous size and thickness. They were breathless, unable
to speak, but only to think about how large the tree was, for they had never seen something so
large.
When he finally let his breath out, Andy said, “How does something so awesomely
enormous form and grow? How is this something that we have in this world?”
“It has been around for thousands of years, since before the great lake was removed from
the land, ah, but the sun helped it grow taller, and we thrived along with it. We have nurtured
this tree since it was first discovered, and we live with it, we are tied to it. You see, if any of us
leaves the forest, we will die, and fade into dust.
They both fully broke out of the trance they had been caught in. They were now curious,
as to how they knew about the outside world. If they could not leave the forest, how did they
know about the outside world? Could they have some sort of tactic to spy on the world?
When Nia asked, a red spirit moved forward, “We, the Ashenoak are not like the
Oakenash. We are able to leave the forest when we please, but we cannot bring ourselves to do
so. They have to get us out into the world somehow, which is why we have the king of the
Oakenash. He was formed when the lake drained, and the forest grew. He has protected us ever
since, or at least to the best of his abilities.”
“What about those we heard before we got all the way here? We heard laughing similar
to yours.” Andy asked.
“They are ones who have been claimed by the fire of Ilat, the fire that hides within this
forest region. He is why the Oakenash king formed, to hinder the fires' evil influence. If he had
not formed, this land would be overcome already by the ash. We would not be alive.”
“What exactly is this fire? We have heard of it several times. And how are you alive?” Nia
asked, as confused as Andy was.
“As I said before, we are connected to this forest, we cannot leave it or we will become
dust, this is in effect until the darkness in the region is vanquished. As for the second question,
follow.”
As the Ashenoak led the way, they followed him to the base of the Tree, “This tree, we
call, Faranlas.” The Ashenoak explained also, that the tree was not only the oldest tree ever to
live, but the largest, and held more life inside of it than the entirety of the land. Andy wondered
how one tree could contain that much life, but then he reminded himself of the size.
They arrived at the base of the tree, though it took them about five minutes to do so. The
trunk alone would be longer than ten of the village houses side by side. If they were going to
walk around it, they would have to walk for thirty minutes. Even though they did not have to, the
thought still gave him a chill. Their destination was above them, in a big hole in the trunk of the
tree. How they were going to get up to it was a challenge though.
One of the Oakenash stepped out of the line and proclaimed a phrase, then there in front
of them, the tree sprouted a low branch and spiraled up to the hole with stairs all the way up it.
Awed once more, Nia and Andy started to climb up the stairs, though none of the Oakenash
followed. The Oakenash dispersed as they climbed, going to their own separate space or
beginning to talk with each other. It became merry in the massive clearing and they were
already singing another song.
When Andy and Nia reached the top of the staircase and walked into the hole, the
branch retracted and returned back to normal wherever it was in the trunk. The tree itself felt
alive with so much emotion and almost a buzzing.
In front of them in the hole, lay a wide open cave, going deep into the tree. It did not feel
like the tree wanted to hurt them, though it did feel very ominous and strange. The cave had a
bottom and they could see the bottom, and even light. The light was greenish and bright. Andy
and Nia knew that the Oakenash expected them to go into the cave, but they also did not know
if they wanted to see what was down there. Though they retained their caution, they decided to
walk into the tree anyway. They were curious, and it might contain answers.
As they walked down into the heart of the tree, they wondered what it was they were
going to see. It could be the Oakenash king, or it could be the store of knowledge. Though the
store of knowledge would be less likely, they did not know what the Oakenash king even looked
like to judge where he might want to dwell. They hesitated as they approached the bottom and
the green became more potent and bright.
They took the last few steps. The first thing they saw was a small green dragon, looking
like it was encrusted with deep colored, dark emeralds. It was smaller than they had expected for
a dragon, but it still looked fierce. Some of the emeralds glowed and reflected off the walls
which were also made of emeralds. The emeralds on the walls were solid, with veins of gold
running through them. In correspondence, the dragon likewise had veins of gold following the
shape of its body.
The dragon's tail was pointed but not sharp. It had two sets of horns and a proud
standing position. Its eyes were a little lighter in their hue than its body and its proportions
matched almost perfectly. Its silvery horns were quite long but matched the dragon's proud
form. The dragon had many neck and head spikes made out of emeralds, though they were not
the horns but rather a part of its neck. Out of its head was an ovalish diamond, centered
perfectly as well as a small amethyst below it. More diamonds dotted its head symmetrically.
And all in all the dragon was perfectly even.
Through its mind it projected a thought, Come closer to me, and welcome. The thought
seemed to wield great force and power within it, as if much knowledge was compiled behind it,
as well as a vast range to its sight into them. It felt like the dragon was staring right through
them. They approached the dragon cautiously, but they did not show it.
When they were close enough to examine every detail, the dragon spoke again, I am the
Oakenash King. You have done well to reach me here, and you were wise to do so. Darkness now spreads
in every region of the land, and our time grows thin. Though I am powerful, I do not wield the power to
leave this forest anymore than scrying on the outside world. I will help you wherever I can, and as I
observe, you are the ones to guide me. I know you think yourselves unworthy to instruct me, but there is
something I know that you can do. Free me from my containment and I will start restoring the forest. The
madness has overcome many of us already, though we still stand a strong kingdom.
You may know that I cannot leave the forest. That is because I am bound as long as there is evil
in my region. It, though, will not come back if it is vanquished. If it gains full power over this region it will
disintegrate it into a pile of ash. If it gains control over the land, it will turn it into nothingness and move
to another land. This, I know, is frightening and a lot for you at once, but please sit and ask your own
questions. Feel free to speak out loud too, I can still understand.
They sat a while, contemplating all that they had just heard and what it entailed before
they spoke. If evil gained grasp over the land, it would disintegrate or be gone completely. It was
a lot to consider, and they wanted all the help they could get.
“How exactly does this evil destroy the land, is it by another opposing creature? Is it just
corruption of the land without a physical cause?” Andy asked with a thoughtful expression.
Its fires take the form of something or thought as well as concept to turn against the people
already living there. Here, it takes the form of illusion and entrances all who are not of sharp wit. If you
made it here without any second thoughts or sitting on the fence about whether you should stray off your
path or not, your wit is very sharp indeed.
“How many dragons are there? What are the eight other regions?” Nia asked, with a
confused look drawn on her face.
The other regions are: Desert, Ocean, Volcano, Tundra, Rock Towers, Great Plains, Mountains,
and the one renewed region. The renewed region is a mistake the fire made, and it gives us other dragons
hope that that dragon prevailed. He was the diamond dragon, and he was the Fires greatest mistake. He
only did something in the name of them being diverse, though it ended up turning against him. The
diamond dragon is the most powerful of us and he is whom we look up to for guidance.
Many more questions and answers were given and received as the time wore on, and the
information was going to be helpful. For the span of their stay, the king had given them a room
nearer to the low part of the tree so they would not have to climb too long of a way to sleep.
They were taught the phrase that would bring out a staircase from the tree so they could climb
into their sleeping quarters. They each had separate rooms and they also had springs that
spurted out of the tree from the ground, but the central entrance was one big hole.
Everything they wanted was provided for them that they needed, so they slept very
peacefully and very uneventful were their dreams. Their minds were clear and their hearts were
content. They slept for the first time without worry of danger, and it had been their most
relaxing day yet though they had walked a long distance.
What they did in the morning was to interact, and find out how the community ran
within the clearing under the tree. They stayed together as they explored, and they made sure to
engage in the conversation with the joyful Oakenash. Even though they were spirit, tree beings,
they still needed regular food to support their bodies, though they could fast for two weeks
when necessary.
Andy and Nia visited most of the shops that were on the ground outside of the tree and
some inside, though buying and selling was not the same here as it was traditionally. They found
that if you wanted to get something of little value, you would have to demonstrate a skill that
you had that was useful yet unimpressive. The same was opposite with things of higher value.
Some of the Oakanash performed for Andy and Nia without being asked, and if Andy or Nia had
seen something desirable, they would allow them to take it without demonstrating a skill.
Andy and Nia found it intriguing to watch the joyful Oakenash sing, and they witnessed
their first dance by the Oakenash too. They were resourceful people who used many of their
skills and strove to learn new ones. Their hearts were light and their knowledge was great, even
compared to that of the dragon.
They had not caught the dragon's name, and if they saw him again, they would ask him.
With anything provided and a joyful company they were content to stay forever, though they
knew they could not. They needed to find the knowledge stored in the forest and move on. They
might come back sometime, but that time was uncertain in the future.
Most of their time was spent in the library. The library could be the great amount of
knowledge stored in the forest, though if it was, they expected something bigger. Even though
there was a tremendous amount of books in the library, the number of books seemed too small
for a ‘great store of knowledge’ as they had heard in the records. Many of the books were about
the outside world, and they assumed that that was their specialty, spying on the workings of the
world outside their pleasant one.
They were satisfied reading books until the fall of the night. As the clearing was clearing
of the Oakenash, something else, a program or a song was going to be performed. It took a while
for everything to be set up, but they were glad for the wait. They had waited for the whole thing,
to witness a play that had apparently been practiced the night before for the guests. The
Oakenash must have had a wonderful memory to only be practiced one time and confident in
front of all the other watching Oakenash and the two guests.
The play was about the fall of the land and the way evil took over. It explained what evil
was and how it created the fires. The main focus was the rise of the diamond dragon and his
uprising. He had found the white fire and it had helped him to reclaim the land. The white fire,
the enemy’s greatest enemy, was why they prevailed in the region.
The play was very interesting and gave the audience a cheery sense, but when the play
was over, everyone retained the mood inspired by the play.
Though Andy and Nia did not know where the great store of knowledge was, they also
did not know what else to do with their time in the forest. They knew they still had a great area
to search, but the space around the tree was big enough that they were not able to just take a
short walk around and find some clue about the knowledge, or could they? They did not know
where it was therefore they did not know if it was just under their feet or some great hidden area
that the Oakenash did not want anyone but themselves to see.
They wanted to ask one of the Oakenash, but they were afraid of what they would say or
think if they were considering intruding on personal knowledge. They had to know, and the
desire was too great to ignore though, so they resolved to ask the next day. Once they had
planned what they were going to say, they would be able to talk without fear, even though they
were already innocent. They were confident that they were going to be alright the next day. They
avoided asking questions to the dragon, whose name they still did not know, because he may be
enraged, and they were not familiar with any sort of dragon. They wanted to discuss a few more
questions with the dragon, so they resolved to talk to the dragon the next day too.
Throughout the rest of the current day, they continued to explore the space that the tree
covered, though they also wanted some time to themselves so they could prepare for the
subsequent day. They set aside an hour at the end of the day to practice and rehearse their
words.
When they went to sleep that night, they were not bothered by anything and they were
able to sleep in peace as they had the previous night. Though that night Andy did have a dream.
In the dream he walked over an empty meadow. The meadow stretched as far as the eye could see
and also the clouds which veiled the sky. The clouds were dark, but not depressing. Andy thought that
they looked beautiful in contrast to the green of the meadow. Then suddenly the scenery changed right
before his eyes. The meadow turned into a grassy hill and the scenery beyond changed to black, as if a
great fire had swept over the land. The sight was not near heartening and it changed his perspective of the
great dream.
A strong wind picked up and it only kept getting stronger with time. Soon he was laying down so
that he would not get carried away as easily. His heart was not racing and his mind was clear, though a
wind could be very dangerous. The grass danced wildly in the wind and the clouds remained, though they
did not lose their dreamy look.
As he watched the darkening sky, for the clouds grew darker indeed, thunder rumbled and
lightning streaked the sky, though no rain yet. He sat, all he could do was sit. Suddenly, lightning struck
the ground and the ground shook too. The clouds began to drop their moisture upon him, it started only
as a trickle but then started to increase in size and weight. It was soon a torrential downpour and he felt
as if it was real, not only in dream. As the great storm progressed, he knew the downpour would not cease
for a long while.
He went to the edge of the hill and decided to look over the land that he had seen before, but in
it. His hill must have been tall so that he could see so much. As he cast his eyes over the land, he saw a
great body of water tossed and turned back on itself. There was nothing holding back the water, but the
water was driven about wildly. As the water turned about, a shimmer ran along its length, and the water
suddenly burst its invisible barrier and started to wash over the land from the east. Soon after, a curtain
of rain washed over his view and blotted out his sight.
The wind flew around him in sporadic directions and without a regular pattern. The mood in the
dream became like the rain in the sky, blue, and like the clouds in the sky, their outlook was dark and
nothing good, now, could come from the land. They had lost.
Even as he sat in the rain, the clouds began to lighten and the rain began to slow. The rain also
thinned and the air was fresh and new. The land was filled with a great mist. That only revealed small
parts of the land. And as he blinked the land was…
He woke up with a start.
7: Great World of Knowledge
It was still night time, he was in his own bed, in his own body, all was normal. Why had
he woken up, what would the fog reveal? What did it mean? He wanted to know very badly,
more than anything. The desire was great and his lust lust was also increasing. He would find
that view someday. He would go anywhere. The sight alone was the most beautiful thing he had
ever seen. He wondered, what did the black land mean? The dream had undoubted symbolism,
though he was not able to define it. He could ask the dragon, though as he thought, it was not
the king that entered his mind. Eradris was who explained, from wherever he was flying around.
I do not know whether or not that did mean anything, though I would not forget it, it was
interesting and you may learn something from it. I would suggest writing it down, and if I were you, I
would not stop thinking about it or pursuing it until you find the answer to your question and great
riddle.
Why did he always receive dreams that made no sense? He would much rather have a
nightmare that did make sense than a beautiful dream that did not have a meaning.
He dismissed it in his mind, but he could never believe the great beauty of the dream. He
decided to take a walk around the great Faranlas, to gather his thoughts. He found that he was
able to gather his thoughts and work through problems better if his blood was flowing and he
was active. The tree took no short time to walk around, so it was the perfect thing for him to do.
He would have plenty of time. He descended the staircase as he uttered the phrase and the tree
sprouted a branch to escort him to the bottom of the clearing.
He paced around the tree. As he paced he thought, or rather let the thoughts flow,
without focusing on a specific thought. He made about four full rounds around Faranlas, and
then he had a random desire to climb up to its top. He stared at the highest possible branch that
was visible and he uttered the phrase that would bring a branch to his feet in order for his use.
As the branch snaked down, to bring him up, from the high branch. He stepped on the branch
and it moved him up to the branch he had seen. From there he climbed the branches.
Climbing though was very difficult in the tree, for there was such a height to it that he
had to climb for nearly a half an hour to reach the top. The ground now was far below them and
now for the first time upon entering the forest Andy saw the pale glow of moonlight through the
leaves above him. He was eager to see the cover of the forest and the great distance it stretched.
As he pushed his head through the branches and out over the canopy, he beheld all the distance
as well as the great expanse of land they lived in. He looked out over the land, though the view
was not as grand as he had seen in the dream.
He sat and stared at the sight for many minutes, trying to take in the entire forest.
Faranlas and its canopy alone stood a few hundred feet above the other trees. He wanted to
protect the great forest. He wanted to save all of the land.
Wherever could Liam be in the grand world? He was somewhere, though also seemed to
remain a mystery as to if he even remained. Andy’s knowledge of the world was not a great
amount of knowledge, neither was it broad.
Andy was going to have to share this image with Nia, she would be pleased to see it with
her own eyes and feel the same things as he did. If she was going to see it, she would want to
know what else he had seen, and maybe even his feelings when he was up there beholding it.
Their main goal for the day, however, was to ask an Oakenash for directions or
permission to find the great store of knowledge. They liked the forest, though they knew they
would have to move out of the forest eventually, especially if they were going to save their land
from the horror of the Black Fire and his terrible wrath being unleashed upon the land.
As Andy returned under the canopy and climbed down into the forest, he thought about
what they were going to do that day and what they needed to do. If they were going to make the
most of their day, they had to start early. By the time he reached the bottom of the clearing
below, he was already seeing others moving about and the Oakenash were also doing their
strange way of business.
Andy went back to the tree cave and greeted Nia who was already ready for the day and
reading a book from the library that was in the forest. Andy promptly went to his own quarters
to dress and to get ready for the things that awaited the two. If they were going to make a good
impression, they were going to have to be early to their plan as well as planning which
Oakenash they were going to ask specifically.
They were ready to go in a short time and they were eager to find that which they had
been looking for. They needed the information, and they knew of no other way to obtain it. If
what they were seeking for was there in the store, they would have to leave quite soon after.
They realized that they had not interacted much with each other as much as they had when they
had been outside of the forest.
Andy decided not to share the thought of the canopy and the beautiful night time world,
but he had decided to wait for a time when they had the time and were not rushed.
As they went to find someone to talk to about the knowledge, a messenger ran up to
them to tell them that they were wanted to visit by the king.
“For what, I do not know, but it seemed important and urgent.” the Oakenash had said.
When Andy’s and Nia’s plans did not go as planned, they were disappointed, but they
dared not risk denying the king. They did not want to miss any important details either that the
dragon might have to share with them. Even though everyone in the forest respected their
choices, they might as well do as the dragon desired, and he did contain more knowledge within
the big head of his. Andy had wondered what it meant if a mind felt as it did with Eradris, and
even more so, the king.
As Andy and Nia made their way up to the main hole that the dragon dwelt in, they
discussed the memory Andy had seen, and why Andy had been gone when Nia woke. If Andy
and Nia were uncomfortable with meeting the dragon again, they composed themselves well.
They liked talking about their troubles and memories, and they had not had a conversation
compared to the one that they had at that time, for a long time. They both agreed that it would
be a good idea to talk about their troubles and thoughts more regularly.
When they reached their destination, and the entrance to the dragon’s cave they walked
in for the second time to discuss what they may. They did not have a foggy idea of what they
were going to do or say, but if the dragon thought it was important, it must have been. They
both wondered what this important, urgent thing was, so they listened to the dragon explain it
patiently, and they were intrigued indeed.
We have discovered that the diamond dragon has many troops marching against his armies and
he calls for reinforcements from the other eight regions. Do you think we ought to send our red Ashenoak?
If so, then I need you to advise me on how many.
“You would ask us? If that be the case we will do what is necessary. We should conserve
our Ashenoak, but as soon as the battle with evil here is done, we shall unleash our forces. How
many Oakenash are still living under protection?”
There are about one hundred and fifty thousand, and there are about two thousand that are the
red Ashenoak. I like the idea of waiting, but we have to hurry to get the evil taken care of. I can tell you
what the fire did to this land, but you cannot utter it to any of the Oakenash.
They were baffled by the tremendous amount of Oakenash there were. They knew there
had been a lot, but they never began to hope for that many.
Do not look so surprised, there are many warriors elsewhere, and a majority of them siding with
the shadow fire. We know of at least two million warriors on the fire’s side. We combined as nine regions
wield almost three fourths of that. The fire uses thought trickery to accomplish its evil and takes its form
as a bear in a cave in the forest
“Well, that is quite the amount, how does one acquire that many?” Nia asked, still
unacquainted with any sort of battle. She was brave though, and Andy knew she would do
whatever was necessary to stop the fire.
Nothing else with as great significance was discussed when they were meeting with the

stole the great amount of knowledge they had stored. They planned to organize a company of
Oakenash to help them along their way. If they strayed from their planned course, the Oakenash
knew the forest well enough to help them out of any situation.
They would start searching for the fire on a day when everything was prepared for their
search. Things they might need in case of a fight, food, and other necessities. They would be

emergency.
When she picked up the weapon, she hefted it and said, “I have never wielded the
weapon, but now that I do, I realize how hard it will be to kill. I will do whatever is necessary for
the safety of this land. I will protect it with my life.” Andy was impressed with her, and he did
not even know why he was surprised yet again. She often said things like that, and she always
meant them. Andy had never known her to lie to anyone at any time.
Andy had practiced with the blade and knew how to use it fairly well. He had not killed
something with it yet, and he was sort of eager to do some good with the blade, even if it meant
killing another animal. He should put his skill to some use instead of leaving it to fade out of his
memory. He was eager to see more of the forest though he knew there would be danger.
In the time that remained in the day, Nia and Andy walked together around Faranlas and
talked, as well as preparing to leave it. The Clearing was in an excitement that night that Andy
and Nia were curious about. They decided to ask one of the Oakenash.
“It is the celebration of Faranlas, much fun and great pleasure, everyone brings a piece of
a song that they can integrate into the entire song. That is how all of our songs are made, we
each bring a piece. Lots of and many wonderful things to drink and much excitement.”
As they waited for the festivities to begin, they sat at the entrance of their rooms and
passed the time by talking to each other. They talked about what-if’s and reality too, but they
pointed out something that was being set up by the Oakenash every once in a while and talked
about what it might be for. There were many strange and unfamiliar things that Andy and Nia
had not seen before.
Some of the Oakenash offered to entertain Andy and Nia, but they wanted to be alone for
the time being. They wanted only to talk to each other about the things they had seen and felt.
As they sat side by side, a thought occurred to both of them at the same time. What are the other
regions going to do with the evil spreading in their kingdom? What was the tactic the fire used in those
areas?
They looked at each other and asked at the same time, “How will we get the other
regions saved in time?”
They chuckled and Andy let Nia answer the question, “If they are now being taken over,
what is it that we can do?”
“We can only get to one at a time, now we need to focus on saving this land. It is the
closest to being taken, for the enemy has found a way to bypass the gap in the forest’s defenses.
Though, I know you want to save all of the land, it may not be possible.”
“Hmm.”
If she had anything else to say about the topic, she did not want to discuss it further, or
she was capable of resolving it on her own. She went back into the room and Andy made no

They were unprepared for what lay ahead of them and they were going to need to gain

Andy had an idea on how they would go about saving the other regions, but it could

could save the other regions and find the dragons, they could have the hope and strength
needed.
Andy stood up, and uttered the phrase that would sprout a branch for his descent, and
then he went to see what progress was being made elsewhere. He wanted to do something, or

make it for some use. He walked around until he found something that he was intrigued by, and

As Andy labored beside the Oakenash, they talked, and Andy learned more about the

though he did not know much about the history.
Andy and the Oakenash contained and controlled the animals into a coral, as well as

a difficult task to get all of the animals contained. There were chickens, cows, pigs, and also
ducks. It was good to see so many animals get along in one pen.
When the Oakenash no longer required Andy’s services, he dismissed him and he went
back to the tree to check on Nia. Andy also wanted to sleep, for it was getting late. He could feel
it in his limbs and his eyes were already closing themselves. Once he reached his quarters, he
went to bed and slept well after checking to see if Nia was still awake. Since she wasn't, he went
to bed without a second thought. Though, as soon as he went to bed, it felt as if he woke up the
next second. What? I only slept a few seconds. How did I just wake up? Ugh.
Andy got out of the blankets on the soft mattress, and walked to the entrance to the tree

and space was taken up by things and displays that could catch the attention of other Oakenash.
They were to display the best of the best of what they had accomplished the previous year.
Impressive things were laying about, and they were also working together selflessly.
Andy went down to greet Nia, and she greeted him with a smile on her face, much

the Oakenash. He also did not want to appear weak in front of them.
As they worked to make the finishing touches to the party, they did not interact much

The festivities were greatly enjoyable and the food was very good too. The food was

Nia enjoyed herself too and they both engaged deeply in light conversation with lots of

During the eating, performances, and the many activities surrounding the tree, a thought came
into Andy’s mind.
The thought was that of little concern. He thought about what he wanted to do afterward
and why the Oakenash were so happy. He wanted to be like the Oakenash, because he realized
he was not as humble, joyful or thoughtful as the Oakenash. The thought was brushed away as a

Andy was very happy at the end of the party, or so he thought it was the end. He asked an
Oakenash, and it said that the festivities often go on for a week. Andy was in awe. He never
suspected that the activities would continue for that long. Andy thought that the time would

Andy was in the midst of the fight when he realized just how dirty he actually was. He

Andy found her in the midst of the Oakenash and a few of the Ashenoak. When he

“We need some sleep.” he could feel the weariness creeping into his limbs. When they

The next day when they woke up to go and embark on the journey, they met their

Andy was going to try and teach Nia a little about using a sword. He suspected that the

“I will do it for you, and I will do it for the land, but I do it because I love you.”
He smiled at her and he promised himself he would not let anything happen to her. He

When they departed, they ended up one person short. The Ashenoak was needed for

When they had made it a good distance away from the forest, they pulled out their map

As they walked along, they made conversation with the Oakenash whom they traveled

They were walking for about three days before they finally found what they were looking

smell reeking from the cave. There were also many bones scattered around the entrance. They

a bear would get, even if it was an evil bear. The animals only made the smell worse. They all
prepared for what they were walking into.
As they walked into the dark cave, they realized it would be deeper than they thought.
They were assuming it would just be a regular bear den, but as they went farther, they needed

only gave off green light. The light was not good enough. They paused, and with the light they
had, they drew a torch in front of their provisions, and lit it with a quick strike of flint and steel.
Then they continued down the cave to discover what we may. They were not prepared for
this, but they were prepared as much as possible for what they knew they could prepare for. It is
wise to prepare for what you do know and what you don't know. Andy thought to himself. He did not
want the Oakenash to think of him as afraid, but he also knew they were not exempt of fear

Andy decided to ask an Oakenash when this encounter was over. They had to be quiet, or

When they were far into the cave, they came up on a wall, and nothing they could see
indicated a different path.
“There could be a hidden wall.” one of the Oakenash on the right side of Andy said.
“Look around for more clues. We do not want this monster to find us first.”
They quickly distributed torches among the group and everyone started searching

Andy and Nia began looking together so they had someone in case they got in trouble. If

Nia only shrugged. They continued to search the cave and they then came upon a

When they got all the way through the doorway, they saw that there were torches by the
head of the doorway and they lit them.
When they lit the torches, more torches, by themselves, sprang to life all along the walls. There
was no bear, though they were prepared to fight. All of them, including the Oakenash, were
tense for something. Nothing was there, only a circular room that held emptiness. It did not
even look like there was a speck of dirt in the room; it was polished stone, and looked pristine.
Something was not right, or the bear that was here was off causing some different kind of chaos.
They searched carefully for traps, but none of them wanted to go to the center of the
room, for they all suspected something, Andy could feel it. Whatever that thing might be, they
were afraid to do what they knew they would have to attempt.
Then, finally, the one who had led them into the room ruffled in his bag. He pulled out
rope, and, as quietly as possible, he whispered, “We must do what must be done, and we all
know it. Who will?”
Taking a deep breath, Andy said, “I will go.”
“You won’t be going alone.” Nia piped up.
“Okay.” Andy thought that he would not be able to convince her otherwise, or she would
do something reckless while he was in the process of hanging by the rope.
They tied the rope around their waists, and slowly, little by little let them walk to the
center of the room. Nothing happened, but the room seemed to get bigger as they walked,
getting farther from the middle. The Oakenash were ready to pull them back at a moment's
notice.
Then it happened, the room opened when they reached the center and they fell down a
hole. They felt the rope detach from them. How? They did not know. They fell, and fell, losing
all track of time into the dark endless pit, they couldn’t think, something was blocking thought…
They fell forever in the chasm, wondering what the Oakenash thought, even without
thinking. Something was now putting information into their minds. But they did not feel this
way alone, the thoughts grew darker. They started to change. They turned into crazy dilapidated
figures staring down at them, increasing in intensity as they fell. When they suddenly came to
an abrupt halt in their fall.
They could think again for themselves and they were relieved to do so, but something
seemed to be pressing into their minds sorting through their memories without their
permission or will. Usually, Andy had to push the thought to an animal before they could access
it. How was this thing doing this? But then he realized, it was not something, but rather, the
absence of anything that knew their thoughts, or at least knew what they did not know. If Andy
was correct, and it was empty space or emptiness, it could occupy knowledge that he did not
know, therefore able to define what he did know.
How to beat it, how do you beat your own self? Andy thought. He was not able to guess. He
needed to think fast, or something would happen. He had nothing else to do but think. Without
a way to figure out how to conquer the challenge he only thought more and more. He was sure
Nia was doing the same.
They thought for an untold amount of time. They were not able to keep track of the time
so they let it flow by. Trying to think, they knew they would have to think of something that they
hadn't thought about. They had to act quickly if they were going to beat the creature or fire from
doing something.
They were about to give up when they found the answer, if they could somehow connect
their minds to reach farther, or if they could connect to fight with the fire to free themselves,
and then they could help the other. If they had enough power to do so, they could break the
enemies defenses and help each other.
First, Andy tried to contact Nia, which worked. Then, he quickly communicated with her
what he wanted to try. They tried to reach for any living life out anywhere. They searched for a
long time, then they found a bird. They told the bird, thinking the same thing, to find other
animals that they could reach and connect minds with. If, perhaps they got enough, they might
be able to overpower the enemy.
They spent the next few hours, days, collecting the animals, they did not want to risk too
little. They needed all the help they could get. If they were going to beat something that had
stopped the flow of thought, then they would need a lot of power to defeat him. When they
thought they had found enough, they confronted and looked for the enemy. Where was the enemy?
It is inside our own heads. Andy thought. He looked at Nia, then he called the animals. He felt the
enemy trying to keep the hundreds of animals they had gathered out, and it was astonishing at
the same time as it not being so to feel.
It felt as if someone was fighting in his head. The clamor of roars and growls became of
the utmost prominence, but he could not aid the animals. His mind was still closed off from
reaching the mind of the fire. Then Andy realized. He tried to reach Nia's mind, but the enemy
was too quick for him. The enemy fire had blocked the stream of his thought from reaching Nia
like there was a bubble around her contained from the rest of the world.
8: Three Fires
In the fight they were not able to think clearly any longer, but then, something
happened. Andy then felt Eradris find his mind. He helped the animals and they were able to
free themselves from full grasp of the fire. He was able to find a way to attack the enemy back,
though he did not consciously do it on his own, his emotions inspired the attack. He needed to
learn to control his emotions, because he did not do so on his own. He realized then, that he was
quite greedy and closed to input, even from himself.
He managed to attack the burning fire boring into his own consciousness. Because of the
many things that had happened though he did not uphold the attack for long. He was only able
to focus on one of the fires, so he readied himself for the focus.
Several times he felt Nia’s mind in the fires mind as well as Eradris, but they seemed to
be at a stationary, none gaining or losing advantage. They battled in this manner for a long time
they could not count. The time seemed to slow though as well as the thoughts that they had. The
fire’s mind could not be broken, though it was weak. It could resign and back away but it chose
not to, for it knew it could not be broken. They fought and struggled, until finally they felt
themselves gaining the upper hand against the mind of their opponent.
With this newfound strength, they pushed harder, determined to defeat their opponent.
Their advantage only increased, and their progress accelerated. They got into the mind of their
enemy and found nothing there, except for a hint of amusement. The darkness was then swept
away, and the area began to light itself. When it reached the peak brightness and they could no
longer look, they covered their eyes.
When they opened their eyes again, they found themselves in the circular room, curled in
a ball, tied to the rope, which the Oakenash held. They seemed like they had woken up from a
dream that had no meaning. Were they going through the same thing? What did they do?
Andy reached Nia and asked with his mind and voice, “Are you alright?
She only nodded and stood up to see if anything else had changed, as did Andy. He
looked at the room, there was not a hole near their feet, nor was there any other change in the
room. Even as they watched though, the room grew over with moss and lost its pristine look.
Then it appeared as the rest of the cave.
What was the bear all about? There was not even a bear in here or in that vision. Then Andy
thought back to the vision, and remembered what he had felt. He had experienced pain like fire
against himself. Was this what the other fires were? Then another thought came to him, Three
fires, one of the mind, the heart, and the physical body. The revelation was crystal clear, and once
everyone had gotten their explanation, they headed back to the entrance of the cave where they
immediately set off for Faranlas and its great clearing.
They were able to have a very good time since they were all eager to leave the cave, even
the Oakenash, who did not seem to be affected by anything, anywhere. It did not take much time
for them to reach the tree, because of their eagerness, though their weariness was greater. They
had one day to spare for the celebration, and apparently, they had extended the celebration,
because of the defeat of the fire. They celebrated for the next few days, in honor of the ‘heros’, as
they got called.
Even though Andy and Nia were the most revered, the other Oakenash were still
respected. They were able to receive some of the credit, and Nia and Andy deflected the
attention of some of the Oakenash to the Oakenash that had been with him.
When celebrations were done, and they had nothing else to do, they were getting eager
to leave. They were growing wary of what would happen if they waited longer than they should.
The forest had felt very different than it had after they had defeated the fire, so it gave them
determination to free the other regions from the fires. If they could free all of the land, they
would have the combined power to be able to defeat the enemy.
They met one last time with the dragon king, so they could inform him of their plan and
what they wanted to do when they had freed all the kingdom. The dragon agreed quickly, and
they were relieved to know it. Andy felt cautious about his own plan, even though they knew
how they had planned it. Something felt missing, as if he had lost something, or shed something
of himself. The feeling pestered him for the remainder of the time they were there. The dragon,
though felt like it was an improvement, though he felt contrary.
They talked about many more things with the dragon. Foremost among all of the topics
was about how they could prepare for the confrontation with the fire. The dragon told them a
very important piece of information. He told them that the fire was still capable of taking over
the land even if he had not conquered all of the land. He could still take over the kingdom if one
of the fries found the weakness of the land. This added urgency and stress to their overall
mission. One thing still nagged at Andy though, was where Liam was.
Although the dragon did not have the answer to his question, he did have advice on how
to go about finding Liam. Also the dragon advised that he should be careful with the desire for
revenge because it was easier for your emotions to go out of control. If he let his emotions run
out of control, they could control him.
If you do not control your emotions, they will control you. When you leave this area that is freed
from the fire, you will be far more tempted. If you give in to this horrid temptation, you will not be able to
see or think clearly. If you die, then who will save us?
The dragon's words dug deep into Andy’s mind. He thought about what he would be like
as a servant of evil. If he were to let the seemingly small things of emotions entangle him, he
would be consumed, and the thought made him close his eyes. He felt Nia also contemplating
that which was said as the dragon patiently watched them. The dragon did not make a sound so
they could have enough silence to think, though the silence did not last.
An Oakenash emerged from the passage behind them saying, “The provisions are ready
for your departure. We await you.” the Oakenash said, directing the last words at Andy.
He will be there shortly, The dragon said, projecting his thoughts toward the Oakenash.
The Oakenash nodded and left.
One last word, if you do manage to defeat the enemy, I want you to return her someday. Also, I
want to know when you find the other dragons. With those words, the dragon pulled out a small
mirror and handed it to Andy. This will allow you to talk to me whenever you want. Your falcon friend
can also give you advice.
Andy wondered how he knew about Eradris. Then he remembered, he could speak with
his mind like Andy and Nia could. When they finally left, and there was nothing else to be
discussed, they left the tree. They were ready to leave, no other extra preparations they needed
to take care of.
When they got to the edge of the forest, they knew they would have to decide which
region to go to next. Andy decided that they would talk about it during their walk that awaited
them.
When they set off, they were wary at first that the confusion and weariness would return
like it had been when they originally walked through the forest. As they walked though, they
quickly realized that the forest would not besiege their minds with confusion any more. They
also knew that they were going to be safe as long as they remained in the forest. The calm of the
forest had them on edge, though, something felt too good about it.
They needed a plan so the main point of their conversation was on the region they would
go to next. The dragon had them look at the map and memorize it so they would not need the
extra weight though it be a little. They thought about going north, straight out of the forest and
back through the rocky region, but as they thought about the scale of the regions, they decided,
