over a long period, that they were going to head to the east toward the tundra, which had
become the largest region in the land.
The tundra had been spreading, and they were unaware of the cause. If they were going
to find the cause, they wanted to find it in the tundra first, to stop the spreading of the cold.
They did not know where the origin of the cause, and their enemy, was coming from or
how they would find the fire. They only knew that, the more power, the greater the advantage
would be so they could stop the war.
From the dragon, they also learned that there were rumors about another enemy that was
smaller, though he discarded it as another servant of the enemy. If he discarded it, that was a
good sign that it probably was not. They still had to remain careful, for if they forsook their
attentiveness, they would probably suffer the consequences for their ignorance later.
One lesson that Nia and Andy talked about as they went along their way was that of the
other dragons. What were the other dragons, and what were they made of. There were plenty of
other topics of conversation, but they seemed to focus on those ones because they were of the
utmost importance. They also had much more interest in the topics because they did not know
as much about them.
On the next day that they traveled, they were given more of the re-energizing foods and
beverages. They did not talk to anyone about what they planned to do but themselves. The less
people knew about the plan the better. Andy and Nia thought that they had to be as secretive as
possible because they had knowledge that no one else had. They also had to worry about
protecting the knowledge they now possessed. If they were going to do that during their travels,
they had to start now to keep quiet, even to those whom they trusted. They had so many
responsibilities already, and they knew they were going to receive more.
Before they had left, they had talked with the dragon about how the armies of the
Oakenash would move out. They were ready to move, every one of them, so they marched to war.
The dragon even left his protection to lead the armies through the forest and into battle. The
dragon's reasoning was that it could intimidate the enemy. If they could convince the enemy
that the dragons were enough to challenge them, then it would be to their advantage and to
their benefit. Andy and Nia even grew their own confidence, as well as inspiring their eagerness
to grow further.
“When we reach the edge of the forest, we should be able to head quickly to the east and
it should go fairly smoothly. If we are not there by the time winter actually arrives, we could be
in trouble.” Andy commented.
“How deep do you think the snow will be in the tundra?” asked Nia.
“I would guess that there will be five or so feet there. Though I have never been there, I
don’t want to be caught unprepared.”
“Yes, I do not want to be taken by surprise,” Nia stated, then after a pause, “We need to
pay attention to the things that we learn. If we forget them, we could be in trouble.”
Andy only nodded and pondered all of the things that he had learned, and the knowledge
that he gained. She was right, if they forgot the things they learned, they could lose something
that they would need later. The other dragons may also want to know information from the
other regions, and the goings on.
When they were at the edge of the forest, the Oakenash bowed to them and left without a
word. When they were prepared to go, they left the forest and did not look back to the green.
They were on the rock that they were before, but this time they were by a mountain. They were
still on the rock because they wanted to see more of the world. If they traveled along the side of
the forest, they would be able to navigate better. They now were by some mountains though, so
they were further east of where they had been when they had encountered the rock giant.
They were on the right side of the mountain and close to the diamond dragons region.
They knew there would be battles everywhere through the land, so they had to be cautious and
travel smoothly across the land.
With many miles to go though they had to focus on the journey. Nothing went amiss on
the first day of their walking, but they were able to spot a group of marching soldiers of about
fifty or more. Luckily they were able to find a nearby patch of trees.
“If we go over there and get low to the ground in the brush, then we will have to wait. If
we do not get down soon they will see us. We are on the top of the hill in the middle of this
meadow. We have to go soon.” Nia said, thinking quickly.
“Right.” was Andy’s response.
They made their way over to the trees to hide and by some luck, they were not seen. If
they had been seen, the soldiers did not pay attention. They had found a perfect place to hide
and they were able to secure themselves without noise, or very minimal noise. They waited for
the soldiers, whoever's side they might be on, to go out of their hiding. They had to wait for
about twenty minutes just for the soldiers on foot to pass their position. They passed very close
to them and Andy caught a few of the men talking.
“We ought to get there by nightfall. If we don’t…” the rest of the sound was carried off by
the sound of stomping feet. Then the voice continued, “then we can actually report this great
news…” Andy could hear no more after that point, for the voices got carried away in a small
breeze that started to blow.
The breeze started to pick up as the day continued to wear on. The day seems to pass
slowly in the greater wind. Even though they had a good time, they felt uneasy about what the
soldiers had said, “This great news…” Even though it was disturbing to them they carried on
through the night.
On this journey, they had a tent, for if they went into the tundra unprepared, and without
proper provisions, they would likely die there. They wanted to test the tents insulation
capabilities so they could be used to it when the time came that they needed to use the tent.
That night when they set up their camp, they did not build a fire, in case they were spotted by
soldiers that might be around. It was wise on their part to do this. They had put much thought
into planning for the possibilities. When they were with the dragon for the last time, they had
gotten many suggestions which they welcomed.
With the help of the tent and no precipitation, they were able to sleep soundly and safely.
They had quickly learned over their shorter time in the forest that preparation is always good for
anything you do. Planning ahead of the present time is one of the best tactics for a smooth run
through of any scheme.
The next day, they were able to cover a good distance, but this time, they encountered
multiple groups of soldiers, though they were smaller. The soldiers became more difficult to
evade, so Andy and Nia had to come up with a better action plan.
That night, when they were sitting in their tent, they talked about what to do if they were
out in the open and confronted by the soldiers.
“We need to find out how to appear inconspicuous.”
“I agree, if we are able to appear like normal travelers, we will be able to get through
them just fine. We need to organize our packs in a way that they don’t look like they have
uncommon tools in them.”
“We also need to turn our course to the left, we have been traveling due east, we need to
travel up. The tundra is above the mountains, which means we could also encounter snow there.
If we are to make it at a timely hour, we need to hurry.”
“If we make it to the mountains still early in the season, we can make it through more
easily.”
Nothing was said for a while, so they sat in silence for a time. They had to keep to
themselves. A lot was said at once, and they had to have some time to think about everything, as
well as incorporating previous words that were said.
Nothing else during the night was said, but they were soon sound asleep. They slept well
and they had no dreams that were uncomfortable. They had much of the night taken up though
before they went to sleep. They had to set off early in the morning because they had to make
better time if they wanted the best advantage to get through the mountains. The mountain snow
could be difficult, and they wanted to get through as fast as possible.
Andy and Nia knew they would have a very tough time getting through the mountains,
so they came up with a plan. They would travel early in the morning for the next three days.
They also needed to find a way that they could get to the other side of the mountain without
being trapped in the pass.
The next few days would be difficult as they drew closer to the cold climate, though they
also knew that that was where their goal was. They made good time the next few days, and they
could see the mountains by the morning of the seventh day of their travel, though they had to
evade soldiers the whole way there. The soldiers seemed to be getting more prevalent and in
their way. The soldiers' numbers also seemed to increase. Increasingly careful and cautious was
the way they had to be.
They were spotted by soldiers a few times, and the soldiers found the spot where they
had been, but they were able to evade them as well.
“Three more days of this.” Andy said as they ran into a patch of trees.
Nia did not respond but she knew it was a tiring prospect for her as well. If they did not
reach the mountains the day after next, they would likely have to trudge through snow the whole
way. They also needed to consider what the snow would be like in the tundra. Their expectations
were high for the condition of the climate, but they knew that they could never know until the
time came. The tundra might not even have a lot of snow, but perhaps more of a chill.
For the rest of the three days, the encounters ceased, but they did directly come across a
group of soldiers on the path at the end of the day, so they had to act like regular travelers. They
got through fine, so their relief was high, and they got to the mountains swiftly, pushed by stress
of getting out of the range of soldiers.
When they arrived at the base of the mountain range, they had to travel a short distance
west to find the most open pass. The pass was the widest, and fastest, but it was not the safest.
They could see snow over the mountains and clouds covering the tips of the mountains, while
small snowflakes glittered in the sun rising in the east. It was a rainbow of snow.
They moved through the mountains, though they expected the snow to increase in size
and quantity. They were relieved though to find that it did not for the first few hours. After
noon, the snow started picking up and the clouds were on the move. They could see the clouds
glancing off the mountainside and blowing in odd shapes with the mountains.
The clouds looked soft against the rigid mountains, and the mountains were tall. They
were in awe and looked at the world in a way that it can do good and bad things to you, but only
if you allow it to blow you away for awe or for despair.
The rainbow quickly disappeared and the clouds moved in from the north. They needed
to move. The clouds promised much snow, along with the temperature that was decreasing.
With the clouds on the move, and the snowflakes getting larger, they had to make the best time
that they could.
That day, the clouds only grew darker, and Andy and Nia had to find a place where they
would be more sheltered from the snow if it came. The clouds darkened the sky along with the
setting sun and they had to find a cave or a tree that could bear them room. Soon, they found a
tall, thick pine tree that had sheets of branches all over it. They had to take shelter from the
coming storm and this was the best place they could find that they could be safe.
That night the snow surely gave way to snowflakes as big as the palms of their hands.
The wind blew softly, and when they looked out over the land, there was a dampened silence
throughout the land, and no sound except for that of their own breathing. The snow was white
compared to the spires that it surrounded, the tall conical spires of trees. The trees were not
rough or rugged, but they were pointed and looked to be soft.
The voice of the wind,
the touch of its hands
Throughout the lands
By another, twinned
They found a dry cave that they could spend their time in for the night. It was not very
large and it did not go back very deep, so they thought they would be fine inside, at least they
would be safe from the cold. They set up their sleeping area, and they were relieved to have the
forest around them as well as they could build a fire in the concealment of the mountain. They
had to chop down a tree so they could have a fire, but they made sure it was a dead standing one
so the fire would not just smolder. They did not want to wake up to the smoke in their eyes and
mouths.
They did not have any disturbance that night, rather they slept better than they had in
the past. Outside the cave, there was about six feet on the ground and it sloped to the entrance
of the cave they had lodged in. The snow was very wet, and taller than Nia was. Andy was quite
tall, though he thought that even the snow would be taller than him or slightly cover him. Andy
knew they could not risk going out in the snow, at least not very far, so they would have to stay
there for a time, as long as the snow was staying.
Preparing for the time that they would have to stay in the cave, they brought the wood
into the cave as well as boiling water that they would need to survive in the cave. The boiling of
the water would ensure that any bacteria would be killed and the water safe to ingest. They
continued to do this for many hours so they would have the best chances of surviving.
They had to sit and stare at the snow for the rest of the day except for tending to the fire
and going back to the fire to get more water. They would get the snow in the pot, boil it and take
it out to sit in the snow so it could cool down enough for them to drink safely without burning
their mouth.
Nothing great was accomplished that day because of their delay with the snow. The
snow would be a great hindrance to their journey and Andy guessed they would be stuck for
about three days. The time it took for the snow to melt could be longer or shorter depending on
the weather, and whether the sun was out or not. They were able to do nothing or make any
progress until the snow melted enough.
They talked to each other and made up different stories to entertain the other as well as
themselves. They also made up small games to entertain themselves, until those got boring to
them, then they just sat. With a few of the pine branches and sticks, they started to whittle with
knives they got from the Oakenash.
Andy was carving an intricate fire with his branch that twisted around a ball of water,
while Nia was carving a strange, yet detailed bird. They did so until they were ready to sleep and
until they went to bed. They made good progress on the creations, though not much of the snow
had melted before the sun retired for the night.
Poetry was flowing through Andy’s head that night as he slept silently.
Fire in the sky
Fear me not to lie
Be thou not so shy
Cease your mournful cry
For the fire in the sky
Will only make you fly
Trials are my glory
Stocking you with inventory
Giving you the tool
So act not like a fool
While fire reigns the sky
Something to remember by
Not in fire
Your desire
Fuels your eternity.
Make me not
But do not bake me
Give me strength without support
And let my stranger age deport
For let my freedom flow you must
It is my greatest, strongest lust.
When they woke in the morning, they had hope that the sun would be able to melt some
of the snow at a good rate. They needed hope in their stuck position, for if they lost hope, their
courage would also fail. They must maintain good hope and strong will. Without hope, they
would be able to gain the confidence needed to travel when the snow was melted.
For the two more days, they whittled, stared at the snow, and otherwise, repeated the
same process they had the previous day. The days dragged on with exceeding slowness, so they
quickly grew eager to depart from the cave. Though the snow had melted a few feet or
compacted with its own weight, it was still up to their torso.
When the snow finally melted to two feet, they made their way into the depths of the
mountains the next morning so they could make the best time possible before the snow
returned.
Luckily, for them, the snow did not return the whole time they were in the mountains.
The rest they had gotten from walking, though, was good for them, and their legs were refreshed
with the energy they needed. They walked a good distance before the end of the day. They were
used to the walking now though, so they did not have a problem walking the distance. They still
had to get out of the mountains so they could find the dragon and ally him with the other two
dragons.
One thing they had learned of the dragons was that they could be oblivious to the cause
that made their regions corrupt. Andy and Nia were there to convince them and their army to
join the great cause and utilize their power.
The next two days that they were on the move, they were able to travel without incident.
They had to clear places for themselves to sleep, because the snow was still resting on the
ground. They also had to be careful and watch out for fake drifts. Streams that come from
melted snow and melt the snow so that there are little caves that can trap someone completely.
9: Stuck Desire, Burning Fire
They finally got out of the mountains, though forest still remained for them to navigate.
They believed that the tundra was just at the other end of the forest, and the temperature
seemed colder than it was at any other time, even in the mountains. The ice in the forest seemed
higher than it was in the mountains and it could be that they were closer to the tundra than they
had been before. They knew not what they would find in the forest, though they did know what
they would be up against in the tundra at least for the weather.
Andy and Nia traveled through the forest with speed and they saw many different types
of trees but they did not see anything disturbing until there was a frozen spike the size of one of
the trees surrounding it. The spike had decimated the trees around it and thrown them out of
the clearing that was made by the impact.
They examined the spike that was pointed to the sky, but they found nothing that told
them about its origin. The spike was very solid and seemed to be created by no natural cause,
though it seemed too fine and clear for any human hand or skill with magic to make. They gazed
at the spike a little longer before moving on and asking themselves what it could be and what it
was there for.
They came across more spikes that were alike to the one that they saw in the clearing,
but none as big or clear as the first. The spikes though did have more detail to them as they
progressed. They did not spend as much time looking at the spikes as they did before, though
they still tried to observe as much as they could.
The snow was becoming greater in height and quantity so they knew that they were
drawing near to the tundra view that awaited them. The snow would not melt as fast in the
colder conditions, even if the sun was out. They also had to be mindful of the things they would
need to get used to out in the tundra that they had chosen to travel. The tundra could pose many
challenges for them, so they had to be aware of their needs as well as knowing their limits.
The forest was bigger than they had thought it would be, but they knew they were still
near the cold wasteland that was their goal.
The falcon, Eradris, was still going to follow them into the tundra, but he was also going
to try and scout ahead of them as they moved and report the snow level, any danger, or anything
else that could hinder them. At least they would be prepared for anything that came up on them,
whether they had to fight or not. They needed to move fast in order to reach their goal with
flexibility. They had lost time in the mountains, so they planned to make it up, and they had also
had a rest during their time in the mountains.
They got to the edge of the forest by afternoon, but they still had farther to go, for there
was another patch of trees, so secluded on its peninsula surrounded by snow. The trees there
looked sickly and dead though, with small patches of leaves in the canopy. The Oakenash gave
them warm coats for their chilled travel, so they could stay warm and get as many miles as
possible and with as little convenience too. They still had inconvenience getting through the
snow, but their progress was still very far for the day, and they were able to get to the sickly
peninsula of dead trees to set up their camp. The trees appeared to be burned in some places,
though they also had many green branches, at least on the bottom of the trees.
Andy was eager to see the actual land of the tundra, but his patience could last until they
did see it. He was not so eager to be cold to go head on into the cold. The next day, his wish was
fulfilled and they looked off a hill into the great vast plain of white. There appeared to be more
of the strange spikes over the plain, but there were also lonely small mountains that broke the
smooth surface of snow. The surface, Andy thought, would hardly be smooth.
As Andy and Nia watched the land, they saw more clouds on the horizon that seemed to
be pushed by a grand wind. They needed to move, and as much as possible so they could cover
the most distance without more inconvenience.
They left the safety of their hill, and walked down the small slope before them. The land
would not change by but a foot as they walked, but they wanted to be prepared for the things
which they needed to face. They made it down the hill and found that there were more spikes
than they had originally thought they had seen. The spikes turned into odd shapes and became
more odd as the day progressed.
They walked the whole of the day and stopped at night when they found a small drift to
camp by. They had to make a fire for their food, but it did not do much to warm them since they
were in their tent anyway. They slept in their tent, though they had to adapt to the new cold.
They stayed warm enough, but they could feel the other shifting in their thick blankets to find a
warmer position. They got to sleep, though it took some time for them to do so.
Waking up in the morning, the terrain seemed to move from its original shape. It was not
quite so flat as they had seen the day before. They saw a hill in front of them when they emerged
from the tent, though it was not there before. They walked up upon the hill and saw that the
terrain had greatly changed, but these changes were no illusions, and the hills seemed to move.
They felt the movement for a surety when they saw their tent raise up slowly as they descended.
The hills of snow seemed to be in great waves, though no force from underneath the
snow could drive the great force. The force was great and the waves seemed to be in every part
of the once plain. The hilly landscape was not what they preferred, and they had not planned for
something like this. They packed up the tent and were soon off, not to stay in one place for long,
for they would get cold and their limbs would lose their heat.
Andy and Nia seemed to travel farther that day, because of the waves that helped push
them along and speed their pace. They realized this when the mountains appeared closer than
they anticipated. They thought that they would reach the mountains the next day, earlier than
they hoped. The progress filled them with new rigor and motivation.
They proved to be very successful the next day, and the waves had not ceased, so it was
hard to get the rest that they needed. They had to keep their balance throughout the days that
followed because the constant rolling of the waves made them dizzy. It became a challenge to
get to the mountains when they thought they were going to get there. They got to the mountains
at noon just in time for them to eat their lunch. Their meal was small and yet very rewarding for
them after walking dizzily over the land.
They sat for a while resting their wobbly legs caused by the dizziness and eating their
meal.
“That land is fascinating, but I don’t think I can take much more of this up and down.”
Nia said as they ate the meal.
“Yeah, me too, I feel like I am hallucinating.” Andy replied.
They said nothing more and nothing changed as they finished the meal, though they did
notice that they did not want to move from the spot.
“I think it would be wise to stay around this mountain as long as we can.” Andy said, and
then Nia nodded.
They did as they planned, and they were able to carry out the plan to circle the mountain
until they had to leave it. They had to go around the mountain, and the convenient thing was
that they were able to avoid the waves more because the mountain acted as a weight holds down
a flapping sheet. They were able to make better progress in this way of traveling.
As they were walking across the snow, they saw something peculiar. In the mountain,
they saw the frozen faces of people, whose eyes still burned with the lust to escape their awful
fate. What could have done this? Andy thought. His face was painted with pain and he felt their
suffering, plastered onto their faces. He felt the pain.
He would hate to die like that. What cold could cause such suffering. It was the cold of a
fires black heart. Andy felt rage boil inside his own heart, directed toward the evil that caused
this cruelty. He could defeat them, and he could do it alone, he needed no other with the rage
inside of him. They had to work together though, for if they forgot the other, they would regret
the decision.
Andy stared long at the faces, though his pain was great. The faces were in the clear ice
as long as they walked beside the mountain. Andy was eager to get out of the sight of the
mountain so they did not have to bear the glances of the dead.
They did not have to bear the burden for long, though it still pressed in upon their minds
like a great weight that was their responsibility to renew.
Clear your mind, you must not focus on that which you must bear, but also never forget your
burdens. If you forget them, you can never bear them, though eventually we must bear them. If we choose
not to bear them, we succumb ourselves to the temptation that we have no burdens. Bear these burdens
when they are at your turn to bear them, and help those who already bear them. Came the voice of
Eradris.
Andy took the bird's wise advice, he cleared his mind, and Nia must have done the same,
for she took a deep breath and released it. When Andy was ready to put it all behind him, he
moved with more confidence. The land must have known though, for it sent snow down upon
them once more and they had to cover their heads and faces to keep warm. It grew hard to see as
the flurries grew in quantity and the snowflakes bigger. They could hear the whistling wind
through a nearby spike, and the snow flew into their eyes. They had to keep moving though.
They had to squint their eyes to keep the snow out of their eyes and they had to be
careful in the slippery snow. They might have sat down, though their determination would not
allow it. They wanted to go till they could not anymore, though they knew that would be foolish.
They had to find the dragon and his kingdom, but they could not think of where it would be.
Andy and Nia had to sit in the snow, they could not see well enough to keep going and
the snow still managed to get into their eyes. They huddled together and pulled a blanket from
Nia's pack to cover themselves. They covered themselves, and sat on the blanket so it would not
blow away. The air raged outside their protection, but they inside could talk without being cut
off by the wind's fast reaction. They did not talk much, but they did try to collect their thoughts
enough to speak with their minds.
When is this thing going to stop? Nia thought to Andy.
I cannot say. It shows no sign of stopping. In another situation similar to this one, Andy had
called upon animals to aid him in his protection. This time, there was nobody or animals that
would be willing to open their minds to anyone, if they were there in the first place.
They said no more but only waited out the snow, they could feel the snow building up on
the blanket and felt the weight pressing in on them. The weight gained and the sagging also
built up. They had to wait for hours for the snow to stop. And while they were waiting, they had
to eat, for their stomachs grumbled furiously at them. They ate a meal in the most cramped
space they had before, and soon thereafter the snow calmed.
They emerged from the drift of snow that had formed around them, and looked around.
The land seemed to be still without the waves of cold ground. The plain seemed back to normal,
except for the extra few feet of snow that layered the ground. Andy did not know what to do, or
which direction to go, for it looked identical in every direction, snow, and white. There seemed
to be no end to the world of white. The mountain was not visible anywhere, but they assumed
that it was only blending in with the white terrain. The mountain would not have been ignored
by the snow, and neither would they.
They were grateful to be out of the blizzard, though, without a direction, they would
become lost. They could be going the long way and run out of food in weeks, which they did not
want to risk.
“Which way should we go?” Andy asked toward his sister. Andy had no idea where they
ought to go.
“Ask Eradris.” She replied.
Andy did not know where Eradris was, or if he even knew where they were, but he also
knew that wind could have forced him to land. Andy reached his mind outward, felt Nia's mind,
and kept going. He could not sense any life, until Eradris’s he recognized and asked if he could
find them or knew where they were.
I do not know where I am either. I have been looking for a while now. To me, I feel like we should
go forward. As for my finding you, I can see you right now, down on the ground by a snow drift.
Eradris greeted them and landed on the drift. As they went in the direction Eradris had
suggested, a fog started to rise all around them, and it only grew in thickness.
The fog was confusing and they could not see where they were, but they continued to go
forward, only knowing that they ought to keep going forward. They were soon emersed in the
fog and Eradris could no longer see them from above, they had to be guided with his mind. Andy
grew wary of the fog, for he could barely see his hand holding it up a foot in front of himself. He
could not see his feet, and the same silence entered the world as the fog dampened the sound of
their footsteps on the crunching snow.
Andy thought he saw a shadow approaching them in the fog, so he stopped, and grabbed
Nia’s hand to ensure she did not keep going just in case. Nothing came out of the fog, but the
shadow lingered. They knew it was still light outside but they did not know if they dare keep
going.
Keep going, we need to keep pushing forward. Eradris’s voice sounded
Andy and Nia went forward as Eradris advised, and the shadow maintained its distance.
Is it even real, and why would it not take its chance to get them? Andy thought. He reached out with
his mind, though he could not feel anything where the shadow was, or even anything in that
direction.
Andy also naked Eradris, but Eradris was not able to sense anything either. They could
only keep going through the strange challenges that seemed to follow them through the tundra.
Andy thought the challenges would persist throughout the entirety of their journey, but he also
knew that they could find more and more power along their way.
They walked through the fog throughout the entire day, and only recognized that it was
getting dark after the sun had already set. Their sleep that night was undisturbed, despite the
strange shadow figure they saw. They also had no dreams to disturb them that night either
The next morning, the fog had cleared somewhat, but they could only see about five feet
in front of them. They had trouble making out what they still saw, but they had help from
Eradris that was more effective from his view, now that the fog was cleared better. Andy and Nia
walked a good distance that day and they were not encountered by the shadowy figures again.
They were able to walk undisturbed the rest of the day. Since there was nothing to judge by, they
had a hard time deciding whether they should keep going the way that they did through the
snow.
Andy had no idea if they were even going in the right direction or the same direction
they had been the day previous. The snow was deep and had an unnatural white to it. The white
also gave them a chill and they were now lost in the snow.
For a few hours they walked, and they were not seeing any signs of life or even anything,
besides snow and more snow. They longed to see a jut in the ground or movement other than
their own, even if it was the rolling snow waves again. Their wish was granted as they looked at
the whiteness all around them and the fog started to clear. They started to feel harder ground
that did not give way beneath their feet. They could not hear anything, though they could see
the greyish rock. The fog thinned even more and they emerged onto a rock plain, above them
loomed a high mountain that blocked out the sun.
The sun was setting swiftly, but they could still see its golden light to their right where
the mountain cliff fell to a height easy to traverse. They made their way to the sunlight and
started to walk around the mountain, in order to know their direction. The sun set to the west,
and they had been moving as it set to their right, so that meant they were going south. Their
direction was not too bad. At least they were not going back the way they had come, and they
also were going in a better direction than they thought.
They were still headed into the heart of the cold desert. They were able to continue their
journey without the constant fog or blizzard. They were very eager to search for anything else
that could give the two of them clues as to where the dragon of this region was. They walked
around and had Eradris take advantage of the clear view to scout out the surrounding area. They
found nothing that day but what they did find was that the spikes were more prevalent around
them and it is deemed as if they moved to watch them, though they did not physically move.
Then all at once the danger struck again, though it was not harmful from the snow or
from the fog, it was direct and living. The faces that seemed to be dead broke out of the spikes
and transformed into ice spikes without the same size as the ones they came out of. They were
covered with ice and all over them was the ice spreading. They cracked out of their prison, and
started chasing Andy and Nia, though they could not catch up if they ran. The sheer number of
the spike creatures would overwhelm them if they stayed where they were. They had to move.
As they ran, more heard them or sensed them and emerged out of their spikes and came
for them as they moved through the whiteness. They had to dodge left and right to avoid the ice
creatures came after them while they ran through the day. If the spikes continued, they would
not be able to last till night, they would have to run all day to escape the creatures, and even then
it would be a risk.
If they could just find the dragon, maybe he would be willing to help them. The spikes
did not decrease in number, however, they increased quantity. They could not now just look
around and avoid, they had to go through the maze of spikes and everything blended in, which
made matters worse.
They ran till the sun was old in the day and about to retire for the time. They ran till
their legs ached with a great soreness, and their heads were only thinking instinctively. They
kept running even when weariness threatened them. Then they saw something worse above
them as they ran, a great big bird. It was some sort of ice monster, and most likely more
dangerous. They were not the only ones that were afraid, the spike creatures also looked to the
sky as they paused in their tracks. Andy was not able to move, but Nia broke him from the
trance that he was ensnared in by the great bird. They were able to keep moving as the ice spike
humanoids ran away or disappeared in a wisp of fog or snow. The snow beast looked down upon
them, and they saw its giant talons.
The beast then flipped around in the air to swoop down and catch them in its sharp
talons. They kept running, and then as it approached them, they ducked and were able to evade
its grasp. They would have to keep doing so if they did not want to be snatched up and possibly
killed by it. They did not lose their minds completely, but they had to keep running and paying
attention to the bird, as they had seen it was, as well.
They suddenly emerged into an area where spikes were not common and were scattered
scarcely through the snow. The Ice bird continued to dive and they continued to evade it. They
knew they could not avoid the colossal bird for much longer, for it was trying new and more
effective tactics. Then another bird appeared, much larger than the first one. The two birds
seemed to briefly greet each other and then communicate with each other and swoop down to
capture them. They were surely going to be torn apart after they were captured, when they
would be captured.
The birds swooped and they were ready to make their last attempt to evade, though they
knew they would be caught. Then a roar shattered the air, and their eardrums shook with
deafening noise. It was not a caw from the bird but a roar, like that of a mighty dragon. The
dragon then emerged from a cloud and dove swiftly toward the pair as the birds circled for a
time, but then landed on either side of Nia and Andy, the dragon pounded down in front of
them. The dragon seemed friendly, but he was growling at the birds. He was bigger than them,
though he did not seem to be dangerous.
Andy and Nia remained wary, but they could feel the mind of the dragon. It was similar
to that of the other dragon they had met in the forest. The dragon then spoke to them.
You seem familiar to me, but I cannot remember if I have encountered you in person before.
You may recognize a friend of ours. Please wait.
They were still afraid of the dragon, but they thought that if they could show the dragon
in the forest to this dragon, they could perhaps make a good impression.
They removed the small mirror from their bags and they saw the face of the dragon in it,
for he sensed when they wanted to use the object. They held it up as high as they could.
The great dragon bent his neck to look at the tiny thing and saw the dragon in the
mirror. They saw the dragon’s eyes widen, and they suspected that it was because he was
grateful that a dragon had escaped the clutches of evil. The dragon raised his head and spoke to
them again.
It heartens me to know that another dragon is safe and free from the evil. I am one of the nine
dragons, and I protect this region, though my army is not acquainted with your race. They are to guard
the land when others outside of the region get too close. If they fail we are the defense. He gestured to
the birds of ice. They were bound to attack you, they can not control themselves when it comes to this,
for they lack judgement of their own. I now will order all of them not to attack you.
They lowered the mirror.
Were those ice creatures yours? We encountered thousands of them.
Yes, those were a part of my army, though we also have many different warriors that are not made
of ice.
Andy thought to the dragon, If they are on your side, then where is the area that you live?
Where do you set up a fortress or home?
We have a palace, though its inhabitants are few, you would have to ride on my back if we were
to get there in a timely manner. Many people would want to know that you exist, and I am sure you will
be able to help us. If you can, then this region will be free of the power that traps us from leaving. The evil
has taken the form of a great ice creature, called a Franshtia.
The Franshtia are common through the land, and it has become an enormous one that feeds this
land to spread its cold, which is one of the reasons that this is the biggest region. Now that you are here,
we can restore the former glory to the land. But let us waste no more time, we can talk on the way to the
palace.
They climbed onto the dragon's back and asked more questions along the way. The
dragon was happy to answer their questions.
Below us, on the cold snow you walked on, and then below that, there was about six hundred feet
of ice. Before I came to be, this land was snowed upon for nigh on nine months. The snow was caused by
the fire that now dominates this land. It wanted to maintain its hold over the land and this was the tactic
it used. It used the domain of once thriving people into a realm of cold and death. We are trying to save
the beauty of the land before it is lost to the chance of doing so.
We also have to believe in the great prophecies of the land, which there are many about our
beautiful land. You two were foretold by a great someone who I do not know if he still lives. He foretold
that a man not yet of age, and a woman as well would come to the land and restore the hope among the
inhabitants. We have long awaited you, and you can now see why the others would not want to wait any
longer.
Nia then had a question that was also on Andy’s mind. What are the names of the other
dragons, the other one we met did not tell us his.
We are not known apart but together as a whole. You see, we were made for the same purpose,
and so our names are not separate from each other. We, if we ever come together again, would be known
as the Shyfrie Gem, a gem that was of the utmost importance and rarest jewel in the world. In our world,
there was only one of these gems. It rested in this land and the one who possessed it, a powerful old man,
made a great sacrifice of it in an attempt to unite the land. He believed that if he split the gem into nine
pieces, they would adapt. He had to cast a spell that took his own life to make us come to be. When we
come back together, we will become this gem. It will have power to split a mountain if it is remade.
Though this power was cut off by the fire of evil and we cannot rejoin unless we free the other six
kingdoms, and my own.
Andy and Nia were thrilled and awed at the power that they could possess. It directly
opposed the purpose of the evil and it was a mirror to the great power the evil could have too, if
it conquered the land. They did not know how they should reply to the dragon, so they said
nothing more, and the dragon left them in peace.
Andy thought that his mind would explode from so much information, so he decided to
get out the small journey journal they had been keeping, and wrote it there. He was relieved that
he would not have to remember all of the information, for he knew he was bound to forget. Andy
was happy though that he had the information so that he could use it to help the land.
They arrived at the palace after the day was close to its end, and the palace was a great
place indeed. Andy’s jaw dropped as he saw the beautiful, yet very eerie sight. It was atop a
mountain and the mountain was crusted with snow. The palace itself was made out of ice and
glittered in the setting sun. It was a sight to behold. They descended into the palace, above a
great hold that had an opening large enough for the dragon to descend into it, which was where
he landed. He descended, and as he did Andy saw many small figures, however running around
the mountain as well as inside the icy structure upon the mountain.
Inside the hold was a great room that looked smaller than it looked on the outside. It was
decorated with tapestries all around, though it resembled the setup of a throne room. The
dragon allowed them to get off his back and then they were guided over to the back of the room,
in the opposite direction of a set of double doors. There was a smaller door, and they wondered
how the giant dragon would fit through. Just then, the dragon shrunk to the size that the other
dragon was.
This one though had sapphires encrusted onto it that glistened in the light. The dragon
had diamonds on the underside of its wings that shined as well. The dragon’s horns were silvery
like the emerald dragon was, but they were shorter and there was only one set. Its chest and
belly was also silvery, though plated with thick silvery plates. The sapphires on it were light but
they had a deep color to them. The dragon's eyes were darker than the deep sea, and its tale was
a hooked point.
They walked farther into the room where a small waiting area was. It had a couch that
was most comfortable and it was not cold either in the room, though it was made of ice. They
took their seats, got comfortable, and then relaxed for a while as they talked to the dragon about
additional things, as well as the history of the land. They were provided with food and bedding
for the night, as well as a relaxing ambience, from where they did not know, except the
relaxation that it provided. They went to sleep that night with the protection of a kingdom once
again. They were relieved for the relief and also the things which were provided.
They were able to get the rest that they needed throughout the night, and they were even
allowed to sleep in. They did not take advantage of this opportunity greatly, but they still
enjoyed the freedom that they now had when they were not forced to the will of the wind and
snow. If they were going to defeat the fire, they would have to be independent from the other
forces of the world. The snow was the least of their problems, they had to find the great snow
Franshtia. The great giant would definitely be aware of their quest, or so they thought.
Maybe it has yet to find out about us. If so, then we have a valuable advantage that we ought to
use against it, if we are able to do the things which need to be done, in an efficient way, we could make
quick work of the evil in the region. Were Andy’s thoughts as he got ready for the day. He believed
that they could use the possible advantage, but he still doubted the fact that they would be able
to make quick work of the evil. Evil that has made so much progress is sure to also have much
experience.
Andy and Nia were greeted by the sapphire dragon in all his glory outside the door and
they followed him through the rest of the palace and they saw that much of the inhabitants were
actually people. They were delighted to meet actual people, for they had not seen a person for
over a month. The people were ecstatic to meet them too, and apparently, they knew more about
what would happen with them, than they themselves did.
They were very famed among the people though they did not even do anything besides
defeat the evil in the forest, but that could not spread through the land that quick. They were the
only ones besides the Oakenash who knew about the occurrence, and they were off to war. If
they were this famous without even doing anything for the people, then how would they be
greeted as they progressed to defeat more fires and news was spread of them out of the land.
Andy thought that he would not like the attention of being a hero to others, though it was a
balanced sacrifice, for the deed that they would be doing.
They saw more of the palace as well as more of the things that they had at their disposal
to use in the fight of the giant. The sapphire dragon told them something similar to the thing
the Emerald dragon had, that they were in charge, and they were the ones who would advise
him. He said this when they got back to the quarters of theirs and the dragon was small again.
Andy and Nia thought that the dragon was just as serious as the Emerald dragon, but only a
different color, their demeanors were very similar to the other’s.
Andy realized that he was happy to have the dragon's company and help. The dragon was
one of their friends, someone who they could trust. Andy thought that they could get the job
done no matter what it was because of the power they would gain. His attitude had changed
about the dragons’, before, he felt as if they were still something to be feared. He was sure that
some dragons were evil and they were not as the nine were, but he did not think about that
because that time, if there was such a time, was not there yet.
Andy thought that if he could find a way to get through to the dragons all in a friendly
way, that he could help unite the kingdom once again. He also thought that that was the way it
had to be done if it was going to be done at all.
Andy was determined to make it happen and he was grateful for the knowledge that he
was receiving and the support he had as he rested that night.
In the morning when they were to plan the day of their departure, they woke up, eager to
see what the dragon wanted of them. If they were going to leave tomorrow morning they had to
be prepared for the possibility that the dragon would say that. Andy and Nia were not going to
be caught unaware as they had been so many times in the past. They knew the lessons, and they
learned them the hard way.
They were urgent to leave as soon as was convenient and timely. There were no feasts as
there had been in the Oakenash city under the great tree, Faranlas. There was also nothing that
the people would be distracted by whatsoever, there was nothing. After a while of talk and
discussion, they came to the conclusion that they could tell the people and select a few
volunteers to go with them and to keep the mind of the others on what was important to them,
while also having them be aware of what is happening. If they let them know as a public thing,
they could avoid confusion as well as suspicion.
They concluded that they would be leaving the day after next and started gathering the
provisions they needed while they could. Andy was able to pack his own bag and help load tools
into the bags of others’ too. They got all of the supplies they needed on the first day of
preparation. One thing remained to do, though. They needed to practice with their tools. Andy
and Nia were given the task of moving water and throwing it onto the ice creature in order to
melt it. The water would be boiling, so they had to be careful as they performed their job. They
practiced aiming with the water that they had, not heated, though they then moved on to do so
with hot water, which they got good at.
Andy was ready to go after they were done aiming to go help the dragon to defeat the ice
monster whatever it was or looked like. Andy had an idea of how he could strategize if it looked
different or the things it was capable of doing. He doubted very much that he on his own could
survive against it. He was glad that people would be going with them, and they were people! He
was tired of seeing only strange creatures and even though they were a help, the Oakenash.
Andy felt more comfortable around actual people than just beings.
When the time arrived that they were to leave the next day, they left very early in the
morning. The sapphire dragon had picked volunteers among his greatest warriors to come. The
dragon had only picked eight, though they were skilled in almost every one of the tasks that this
race of people had come up with or developed. One thing that Andy was also glad for, were
those warriors.
With their early start and a fresh day, the group of eleven made it a fair distance that day,
but since there was snow, they had a different method of transportation. They used sleds that
had harnesses to a machine that was worked by two people. While one of the people went up the
other pulled down. A handle was attached to either side of a wheel that was large and was where
the momentum came from. In this manner, they traversed the tundra.
It would take them about three days to get there, that was, if the weather held. It held for
the first few days, but then fierce wind blew them a storm. They had a hard time, and the wind
blew directly against them as they went, so they were slowed. They said they could see the ice
giants' lair. The Franshtia would not know what they were capable of. If they could overtake it,
they would regain power over the land.
Andy wondered if the people there had already tried to fight the Franshtia. He asked
them, and they told him that some of the snow birds, as well as the dragon had confronted it, but
they could not carry enough water to melt it, “The Franshtia would only replenish themselves
with more snow, and they could not take its protection low enough before it just gathered more
ice. They also could not see the Franshtia because of the steam it gave off when the water was
thrown.”
“Then what will our buckets do to this thing if the amount he could carry is much more
significant?” Andy replied and gestured to the sapphire dragon in the sky above.
“We are looking to weaken only its feet, and if it loses its feet we have an advantage then
to close in.”
“Oh, what will it do if it can not get back up?”
“It will transform itself into more of itself to try and overwhelm us in order for time to
gather itself back together most likely, but no one has gotten to the point of that.”
Andy nodded and did not reply, but he thought he understood the implications of the
things that the man had said. If they shrunk the giant into smaller versions of itself, then they
would then have to melt all of those. Though it was a dangerous chance of them doing so, they
also had an advantage. When the giant split, they would be able to melt all of its pieces, rather
than all of it at once.
They had brought lots of wood with them, so that they could start fires each night and
keep their warmth. That night they uploaded some of the wood and started the fire for their
warmth and comfort. They slept well that night and they were able to keep the fire going the
entire night. Someone had the duty to get up every hour to tend to the fire as well as check that
nothing had gone amiss. Woke up in the morning so that they could continue his journey,
nobody else was up but him, and he enjoyed the silence that he had to sit around the fire and
think.
Andy had a turn to and nothing was out of the ordinary, nothing at all. He had nothing to
worry about during his hour. Andy was right to think that too, for if he started making things up
in his head, he would start to believe them.
When Andy woke up in the morning so that they could continue his journey, nobody else
was up but him, and he enjoyed the silence that he had to sit around the fire and think.
Andy thought about everything that had happened, though he thought nothing at all. He
was passively contemplating everything that happened during the past few months, as well as
what might happen if their plan went totally as planned. If everything worked out, what would
the land be like, and what would they govern the land as, or who? Andy could not imagine the
responsibility of being ruler over a land so great, but he still knew that sometime they would
have to do something about ruling the land. They would have the power to govern thought,
though that would be a denial of those peoples agency.
He thought about those things until the others emerged from their tents and came out to
join him by the fire. They waited for everyone, including the sapphire dragon to move on, but
they did not have to wait very long for that occurrence. They kept going toward the giant's lair.
The lair was inside a hill that was big enough for ten blue whales to fit in, with space in
between. The giant must be huge! Andy thought as he looked at the hill. If the hill was any
representation of what it contained, then they would indeed be in for a challenge.
They got to the hill the next day when it stopped snowing and they were able to find the
entrance to the lair and they were preparing to enter the cave of ice. Before they entered the
cave, they prepared many fires with much wood so that they could have water boiling when they
got back up to the surface.
10: Song of Ice
As they entered into the cave, they felt the cold envelop them, colder than the outside.
They had not expected that, though as they persevered through the cold, their eyes began to
adjust. A faint glow seemed to be coming through the walls, and the cave got darker. They had
to light a torch in order to see.
The torch allowed them to see into the cave, and they could see that it went down farther
and farther. They pushed on, and as they did, there were passages left and right that they
thought mirrored their faces. All of their footsteps echoed as they walked and they kept walking
anyway. The trek seemed long, and they kept going farther into the ground. Then they came to a
hole that had a descending corridor all along the edges of it. They looked at each other, and then
continued down the hole.
The sapphire dragon was in his small form and he was leading them through the icy
corridor. The dragon was leading them for a while without giving any further direction though
he did make a few motions. Perhaps he feels something and wants to be cautious. Andy thought.
The stairway led them far into the ground and they knew that the giant would not be this
deep, but there could be answers to other questions or something they did not want the enemy
to have. If they could decrease the chances of his advantage, they would, in return, gain the
advantage. In the cave, they continued until their torch started to dim, and that is when they saw
a brighter glow in the dark cave. They continued until they reached the light. The light was
coming in through the wall, and it was a darker hue of blue than the light they had seen above.
They needed to get through.
They had to break the ice to get through, and as they did, a door opened for them on the
right side of the wall. They stopped, looked at each other, and headed to the door. The door was
small, and they had to crouch to get through it, but they saw the source of the light. There was a
fire there. It was blue, and it showed with great brilliance. Andy felt an urge to touch the fire,
but he knew that it would be a mistake
With disgust, the dragon growled at the fire. It seemed like it would be the fire that ruled
the region, but then the dragon projected his thoughts to everyone, If this was the fire, it would not
have let us just sit by idly. The fires all took the form of something or someone. This is not the fire,
extinguish it, it is fake.
The soldiers stepped forward and threw a blanket they got out of their bags, over the fire,
and pulled it down. The fire was real, but it was only opposing the blanket, because of the
convection current. They had to pull down the blanket so that the fire lost its air and choked out.
The room retained the blue light, though the fire had been extinguished, and they were able to
see fine still. They searched around the room to see if there was anything else, but they ran into
nothing that would be useful.
They were heading back to the top of the land when a rumble shook and the dragon
stopped in his tracks, That is him.
Andy prepared himself and they ran up the icy corridor to see what was to be done with
the fire’s icy form. They got to the top and ran out of the cave. They climbed upon the hill to get
a better view of the land. The giant pushed himself out of the ground and then started his ascent
up the hill.
It was as tall as the trees in the forest ruled by the Oakenash. It was icy like the other ice
creatures they had encountered and its eyes glowed with a profound darkness in blue. It stared
down on them. Its feet, the goal of their quest, were larger than Andy was, and wider than he
was tall. It was very daunting to their small forms.
We need not waste more time, we must go now. Now or never! Go, I will rain fire from the sky! He
launched in the air and transformed into his large self and flew to the sky. He then did what he
wanted.
Meanwhile, the warriors and Nia had to maneuver down and around the giant's legs so
they could throw the boiling water they had been heating onto its feet. They did this time after
time as the dragon blew his fire from above. He was to provide a distraction as well as weaken
the monster so he would have to regenerate the damaged parts. Andy was throwing the water
and it seemed to work for the first while, though as they continued to do so, the giant learned
their strategy and they had to retreat back to the hill because he started stomping.
All of them were able to make it out safe, but they were soon back in the fray to further
their attempts. As they were in a good spot, and the giant's feet were almost melted, the dragon
came down from the sky to finish the job, and a good thing too, for they had run out of water.
They were able to topple the giant onto his face as the dragon melted his ankles, and
they then had to move the fire onto and around the giant to melt him. The dragon started to
breathe fire to his head, and the warriors near the giant started hacking away at the giant with
swords, and maces. They, together, made good progress, and when Andy and Nia and the others
arrived with more boiling water they poured it on the back of the giant to try and hurt it as
much as possible.
It could not keep up with their pouring of water, but it did still do some damage to their
plans by using its hands to smash a warrior. They then tried to melt the giant's hands so they
could prevent those further occurrences. Andy and a few other soldiers managed to melt off one
of the hands of the giant and then they moved to the next. They were making better progress
then they had thought they could, and they managed to melt off the other hand by the wrist.
They tried then to melt off the arms of the giant, for it continued to cause damage to
their plans and they got angered by the amount of loss they had suffered. They doubled their
efforts and they managed to melt off one of the giant's arms. The dragon was still blowing his
fire at the head that proved to be harder to melt than the rest of the body.
They were succeeding, they had now melted off the other arm and one leg. The giant
roared, and it gave them pause. They stopped and they had to cover their ears because of the
extent of the roar. The giant caused more damage to the warriors and took out three. They only
had six of the warriors left, nine of them in total still remained.
They started again when the deafening roar ceased. They were followed by another roar,
and then the dreaded happened, as well as something that was to their advantage. Andy could
not choose if it was an advantage or a disadvantage. The giant transformed into many of itself,
even the arms and legs that had melted off transformed into the smaller versions of itself. Some
of the versions were distorted because of lack of enough ice, but they did not know if these
smaller ones could regenerate.
The smaller ones, luckily could not regenerate themselves, as they found out, and they
were melting multiple of the creatures at once. There were so many of them that they could not
stay in the same place. They had to move several times to give themselves room. Their progress
was destroyed by then. They were surrounded by all of the creatures, and they had managed to
keep them on the move long enough to make their fire too cool to boil things. They needed a
better solution.
The dragon could not keep up with the constant amount of the creatures from above.
Andy was there and they had nothing else to do, they got help from the dragon, but they needed
more. Andy reached into his own mind, secluding himself from the rest of the world. There, he
found something he did not expect, there was his power but it felt different. The power wanted
to grow. It wanted to learn. Then Andy thought to Nia, through his mind, Reach inside of yourself,
your mind, what is it.
The creatures closed the gap farther.
I feel it, it is as if it is pushing my mind outward. Something wants me to dig deeper, it wants me
to learn. I feel like it is a constant flowing motion, like water. Wait, water, that is the thing we need that
we do not have.
Have we not? Below us, and all around us.
Yes.
They then knew what they had to do, though they did not know if it had the greatest
possibility of success. They reached together out to the minds of the creatures, for the minds of
the creature thought together. They were able to seclude the minds of some of the creatures
from the world. The ice's greatest weakness was fire, but since they did not have enough of that,
they would borrow from the fire inside of the ice. They were able to find the fire that drove the
minds of the Franshtia, but they had to focus one by one.
They then realized that by secluding the minds of the creatures they also secluded
themselves from all the others too. They removed the barrier and they could then feel all of the
combined fire again. They reached through all of the creatures, and they brought out the fire
inside of them all. One by one, the fire seeped out of them, and they could hear the internal
yelling of the combined force of the creatures’ minds. They extracted all of the fire out of the
creature, and as it accumulated above Andy and Nia’s head, the ones with all of the fire
extracted looked confused.
The Franshtia then fled when they saw the dragon. Andy and Nia realized that the
monster could not create anything but it had to steal its body from the already existing
Franshtia, then they realized that the fire was totally extracted. They withdrew from their
concentration and the fire lowered to hover just above the ground. The fire seemed to swell and
shrink.
Andy and Nia simultaneously took a deep breath. This fire, and all of those that were
spread throughout the regions, held the fire of the mind. This fire was the source of confusion.
The fire could almost imitate a physical form, but this thing was not living, it did not live. They
then realized what the fires were, they were part of something bigger.
They only then realized that the warriors still remaining were staring at them. They felt
awkward under the pressure of so many of them, at least it seemed like there were a lot of them.
Then all of a sudden cheers went up from them, and they walked up to them, not knowing that
they were capable of such things. They were unaware that they were capable of that either, but
then they realized that they had learned.
The dragon joined the fray and let out a roar of his own. It brought a smile to their faces
though it also made their ears ring. Projecting his thoughts to all of them, the dragon said, Shall
we celebrate!
The soldiers let out another cry and Andy joined this time. Just then, the ice started to
wave and they all laughed. With enthusiasm, Andy shouted, “Shall we go do some sledding?”
Yes but what about this fire? The dragon asked them. Wait, I know, we need to extinguish it.
But we will all have to combine our thoughts. Andy, Nia you reach to my mind and I will reach to the fire.
We need to fully extinguish it.
They did as the dragon said and reached to the consciousness of the fire. It was strange
and burned with strange desires, but they knew they had to destroy the desires. They reached
farther into its mind, digging into it and extinguishing the desires it had been given by the other
fir, the original one. When they had extinguished it, the physical fire sputtered and died, though
they could never destroy the memory.
The warriors let out a shout, and they got ready to return to the palace. They let those
with sleds sled down the waves as if they were ice surfing. The ones with the wheel got
themselves up the hill with the system of momentum and then if they fell short, they could push
and pull the handles. The ones on sleds would just use the momentum of the forward moving
waves and the power of gravity to bring themselves to the top of the hill and then repeat the
process.
They got to the palace within two days and then they had a grand celebration. The
celebration was long and took up many weeks.
They had many interactions with the people and got to know them very well. The people
were ecstatic to hear the story of how the fire had been extracted from the creature and what
they had done with it. Andy and Nia were happy to describe how they had done it, even though
they got asked well over a hundred times. They had one of the best times of their lives, and they
chose to enjoy it, letting their worries fade away for the time being as they enjoyed time with the
people.
Andy and Nia had been there for a while now, though they still needed to make the
necessary plans for their next course of action. For this, they consulted the dragon. It advised
them to hold to whatever they thought would be best for them. If they went to the region of
rock, as the dragon had suggested, they could take the rock giant's fire that was within it and
find their way from there. The dragon for the rock region was an amethyst dragon. Since Andy
and Nia could not think of anything that would be better for them, they took the dragon's advice
and made plans to leave the next week.
They made the most of their time in the tundra, and they were able to have a thrilling
time. The dragon even opened himself up and interacted with the others, unlike the other,
emerald dragon. The celebrations continued throughout the entirety of their stay from there on
out and there was food that filled the great hall of landing, where the dragon had first landed.
They tried every food that was provided, and there were many. There were also many
beverages that they were able to choose from. Andy’s favorite was a green drink crushed from
some rare leaves that was previously grown, before the ice spread farther outward. There were
many more that were to his liking and he made sure to taste all of them, but that one by far was
the best.
Andy laughed, talked and thought with the people. He took their suggestions of what
food to try as well as trying his hardest not to start another food fight, as he had in Oakenash
city. Nia, meanwhile, was looking at the animals that were in the land. There were many, though
they had not seen them on their way to the palace before. There were different kinds and sizes of
rabbits as well as different kinds of dogs as well as other things. The animals were strange and
had many different colors. Most of the animals were a color so that they could blend in with the
terrain.
Andy was caught in a conversation with the dragon and they talked about many different
things. They were able to make further plans to help Andy discover more of the possibilities that
he could use with his power. Andy discovered that he might be able to manipulate the properties
of water if he applied himself. The dragon had Andy practice, or see if he could manipulate the
water.
First, Andy tried turning a block of ice into water, but then when he could not perform
the task, he tried to move just water. He figured out that he could move the already existing
water but not change the state of the water, at least not yet. Andy also tried to heat water, and he
found that he could turn the water into steam. He could not figure out what it would be useful
for, but if something came up, he was sure he would remember the power.
Andy had Nia try it too, though she made an easier time of it than he did. She was also
able to do what he was not able to do. She was able to melt the ice block that he was not, and it
left a puddle on the ground.
11: Rumor of Death
When they were done with all the festivities, two days before they were to leave the rocky
region, they were making the final preparations for their leave. They prepared to leave by
stocking with food as well as supplies. They were ready to leave, though they wanted to stay so
they could interact more with the people of the land, and they did not know when they would
see more people, so they wanted to spend as much time as they could with the people.
Nia was tired of staying in the tundra, she had never enjoyed the cold, though she could
stand it. She had many wonders about what the purpose of the things the dragons made her and
Andy do, and she began to wonder if Andy wondered the same things and if he did, what he did
them so willingly. If he was doing it so willingly, without thought, there must be some hidden
reason that she did not know about, that he did not want her to know.
Nia was ready to leave the region, but she only knew when they were going to leave and
what they were going to take. She wondered what lay ahead of them in the other regions, what
odds they would face. Opposition had to rise up against everything that was good, just as the
good had to raise its head against the bad. They knew that if they could defeat the enemy, they
could get their wonderful lands restored and cleansed from darkness.
The next day, Nia got up before Andy had, which did not happen often, no matter what
she did. She woke up and went to talk to the dragon, though she then realized she could just
contact him with her mind. She wanted to know what the other dragons were like and ask more
questions about the Shyfrie Gem. If she could know more things she could be able to go into the
world with more knowledge and power to be able to bring to pass the dream they had for the
land.
When she contacted him, he replied, Yes, small one?
I do not know what to do with my time so I decided to contact you so I could ask a few questions.
What are these questions of yours?
I want to know more about the dragons, just to be prepared, and I also want to know more about
the Shyfrie Gem. What more can you tell me about them?
You want to know about the dragons, very well I will tell you all that you know. The dragons
started when the land was tainted by evil, but they only formed after the evil started and took root. The
dragons, as you know, were sent to hinder the spreading of the fire in any way that they could. What you
may not know is that the dragons were also part of the Shyfrie Gem, but the Shyfrie Gem was found in the
stomach of a different dragon. As interesting as it seems, there is an explanation. The dragons have
something that forms inside of them, a gem, but this gem was created because of some reaction it had
with something the dragon ate. Some say that we could make another gem like this, but we do not want to
risk it. The Shyfrie dust is hidden in the desert region.
What does the regular Shyfrie dust do?
Shyfrie dust will glow, as well as turn into anything that you wish it to, which is why the powerful
man was able to turn the gem into us. I can not tell you more about the dragon's background, but if you
want to know more, please tell me. I will give you a mirror as the emerald dragon had, and you will be
able to use it the same way. If it breaks, though, you may still use the shards, though you will have to be
careful. Do you have any more questions before the day is old?
No I do not, but thank you.
The dragon withdrew, and Nia was left laying in her bed. She heard a shift to her right,
and saw that Andy had woken up. He sat up on his side of the room in his lounge and looked at
her with a disturbed expression.
“Did you have a bad dream?” she asked him.
“No, I had a strange dream, it just had a feeling of urgency to it. I feel like it has some
meaning, I have had it before.”
She looked at him curiously.
“I lay in the rain for a while, then I get up and look over the field of black and then it all
gets washed away by the rain and flood that goes through the land from some ancient time ago.
When it clears away the black it reveals the renewed land and shows me what will be. I do not
know what it means.”
She contemplated what he had said, but she could not come up with a solution to his
dream or an answer as to its symbolism. He even shared the memory of the dream with her and
she could not think of what it could mean.
She thought about the dream for the rest of the morning, and a few vague thoughts came
to her mind about it. They walked around the great palace and learned more about the system
they used for getting food as well as gathering the resources that they needed. Andy, apparently
was not successful either with the answer to the dreams symbolism, but it seemed to weigh
heavily on his mind throughout the day. She had noticed that he did not like to reveal his
feelings, though he wasn’t very good at hiding them. Nia thought that she could hide her
feelings well, though she was often very open with them towards those she trusted.
Nia continued to think about the dream and she was going to go to a certain area in the
hall they had been in, the hall where they first landed. The dragon apparently wanted to show
them something that only was in the ice kingdom, and rare. They did not know what it was that
they were going to see or even if it had a name. When they met the dragon so it could show the
thing to them, they were on the day they were to depart.
It was early in the morning, for some reason, the dragon had requested it to be so early in
the morning. Maybe it was special enough that others should not see it. Whatever the reason
was, Nia was sure it was a good one. She trusted the dragons, but she still held a cautious
demeanor, just in case.
When they got to the room that they were supposed to be in, the dragon reached up with
its talons and pulled a torch that was on the side of the landing hall. A doorway opened up and
he stepped through as his small self. Nia and Andy walked through the doorway after the dragon
and into a small room. The room held only one thing, and this thing was a pedestal with a small
globe. The globe was made out of gold, and the metal seemed to glow with a strange light
The dragon looked at them. Take this with you, do not lose it and protect it, it is a powerful
tool, but do not treat it as a toy. It will guide you if you remember to use it. If you do not use it, it will lose
its power. It needs to go onto a pedestal on the other side of land. It needs to be put there so it can keep
the land in order. I believe it will help you to gain control of the region.
“What is it, why was it created?” Andy asked the dragon.
It was created by a master magician who took it upon himself to set everything right, the
magician was overcome after doing this good deed and his trinket was lost. We were able to recover this
item through a great deal of suffering. There was a war long ago that was fought to gain control over the
other regions, but it went ill for both sides. Before the war was over, we managed to steal this from the
lava kingdom who had stolen it from its original place in the Desert kingdom. No one alive knows that it
still exists but you and I. You must return it to the kingdom of the desert before the minds of the greedy
take hold. Take it now, before anyone can realize that we might be in here.
Right away, they took the trinket and stuffed it into their bags and walked out the door
quickly. One other thing, The dragon looked at them, there has been a rumor swiftly spreading about a
town burnt down because a resident in the town started the fire himself. Do you know anything about
this?
Nia did not answer, she would let Andy do the talking, as she often did. Andy had a
pained expression on his face as he said, “We were in that fire and I believe I know about
someone who started the fire, though his name escapes me. I do not know where he is or if he is
still alive, but I do know that the rumor is true.”
