not their king, but he was very respected by them.
They were escorted to the tree, and it was far more massive as they walked closer, it was
all put in perspective. Everything was bigger than they had seen before, and the tree was farther
away than they had originally thought.
They walked as far as they could, but then they could not go farther, for the rifted hole
that they now had to cross. The Oakenash waited for a while, and then one stepped forward and
uttered a phrase. The phrase made the tree's roots stretch out and form a fancy path that had
arched paths along it. It was long, and the roots dug into the ground when they reached them.
They were made completely out of the branches of the tree, and they were there to help them

They got into the tree, and they saw a massive room that was completely filled with
Oakenash, it emphasized just how big the tree was for Andy. If it could fit so many of them, they
would have to walk for hours to reach the top of the tree. They were not able to get to the middle

Andy was about to get to the center, when they were greeted by one of the Ashenoak, and
the Ashenoak told the other Oakenash to leave, and then the Oakenash did as he said. When

When they arrived after a while of them walking, they were at the entrance of the. Andy
was there and he was not, he looked at the entrance, and it reminded him of the past. He was in
the past, and he was paying attention to the shape it bore, as well as the wood it was made out
of. It was lined with stone, but the rest were natural branches in the perfect shape of a circle.
Andy walked up with the Ashenoak, and he looked at all the details as they crossed the

gem encrusted to the walls and sprinkled with leaves of green.
21: Broken Plans
Andy was to Nia’s side, and Liam was on the other side. Liam thought it was still strange
that they were on this quest, but his parents were gone, and theirs were nowhere to be found.
Liam did not know their story before they got to the desert, and they had only given him a few
details about the dragons that they had met before. They had said some things that gave him
clues to what they saw, but he could never know for sure.
They were in the room encrusted with onyx, and they were looking at a small dragon
with the onyx also encrusted to his side and head. The dragon was proud, but strong, and he did
not have a prideful demeanor like the desert dragon. This dragon looked at them and
approached with small steps that seemed like they were weary and tired ones. They did not
know if they could talk well with this dragon, for it seemed a small portion detached, even
though they could not physically see it.
Liam was ready for what this dragon had to say about them or maybe what they ought to
do, but the answer came slowly. What are you doing here, what is your purpose here? How did you get
through the wall? I have heard some interesting stories about you. I have communicated with the Emerald
Dragon, but he says you were coming to save us, is this true?
We have come to help you in any way that we can that does not go against the cause of us. We
have seen that you have defeated the fire here, but will it come back? Answered Andy.
I do not know if it will return, though we need to keep you on your way so that you can help the
other regions, though we have never been able to get the tornado like that, so it might work to keep the
fire away.
Liam then spoke, though he usually stayed quiet and observant, Why do you limp? Have
you been hurt?
I limp because I have fought the fire when it was in the form of a great bear, and I defeated it,
though I did not keep it away. I also suffered a few wounds.
Is there anything that we could do to help? Nia asked.
Would you be willing?
Of course, Andy and Nia replied at the same time, We would help you, we will need you if we
are to create the gem.
Ah, that is kind, though, you may not know how to help me. Well, there is a spring in the volcano
area that will be able to heal me if I but have a little of it. If you would be willing to help me get that, then
I will be safe again. Thank you.
Alright, where might this spring be? Andy asked the dragon, and Liam wondered as well.
It is almost directly south of us, and it is secluded in a small valley, you may have to search
around to find it.
Alright, is there anything you want us to take with us, or do we go and find it easily enough. Nia
asked.
It should be fairly easy for you to do so, to get to the spring, but just in case you need something
to carry the water in, I have these. The dragon gave them ten small vials that were empty so they

Liam thought that they would be able to get the water easily, at least according to what

They left for the area that the dragon had said in the morning, and it felt good to sleep in

They would have to go over the mountain if they were to take the fastest route, but it

They climbed the mountain, though it was harder than it looked, and Liam expected it,

When they climbed the mountain, they were very tired at the top, but it provided them

They had brought a hatchet, and a few other items for the firewood, but they might even

They traveled for the next few days without being able to see anything, but then at the

They could not afford to waste any time on the water, but they would have to be most

the goblins they had encountered before, though they would have had to travel far after they had
fainted. The others were probably wondering the same, but he dared not ask, for goblins had
good hearing when they were aware, and the sun was setting in the late night.
It was good for them and bad for them, for they had the cover, though they still had to

guard, there would surely be more goblins, but there would also be the water. If they waited any

someone batted down his passion, he would not be happy for them, but he would be mad at
them a great deal.
They walked silently across the mountains until they got to an area that they could safely
descend. They were able to descend swiftly, and the night’s silence seemed to enhance every

near, they had to be more quiet, or at least as quiet as possible.
They were able to descend and get onto the foothills of the mountain and it was easier to
stay quiet from then on. They were there in the moment, not confused, but they also had to be

they would be vulnerable. It was a race against time. If they lost their senses, they would not be

Liam did not know if Andy knew how to use a sword, but if he could not, it would be a

Liam had never seen him use it.
If worst came to worst, then they would have to run, but goblin shamans used magic, and
if they did encounter one, they would have to run fast to escape the power. Liam wondered if
Andy could counter the power of magic with his mind, but it seemed unlikely that he would.
Liam remembered the time with the Wincher Wales that they had fought and won because of
Andy’s strange push back. Liam still wondered what drove him to do that, but he could never
know unless he got into his mind, but if he got in, he would have to gain the knowledge of how
to get into his mind so he could see something he just accomplished.
Liam thought as they ran silently through the woods, then they suddenly got to a
clearing that the lake lay in. They could hear the sound of a nearby waterfall, and not far to their
right was a goblin camp, trying to guard the lake. There were many more, but they were spread

many to fill.
Andy went back into the trees so they could whisper a small plan. They were far enough
in when suddenly, Nia stepped in the wrong place and the sound of a stick breaking sounded
under her feet. Liam shifted around back the way they had come, but he knew it would be too
late. He would need to silently sneak to the water.
“We might be able to deter them and run back out when they are in. If we can do that, we
might just have the time we need.” Liam whispered as he turned back around.
Andy nodded quickly, agreeing to the plan. They ran into the woods, not caring if they
made noise, for they wanted the goblins to follow them, and then they silently turned, and at an
angle, they went back out into the clearing to get the water.
When they got to the lake, they could tell that the water was different from any other
water. They quickly got the bottles that they had out of their bags, and kept their eyes open for
any goblins that might have seen them. They managed to fill all of the bottles except one, and
right then, the goblins came out of the trees. They only filled the bottle halfway, the sun was
down, and they had to run. They did not need all of the bottles filled, for the dragon said that he
only needed a little.
They made their way back to the area that they had climbed down so they could get back
up, but they could not run right past the goblins, they would have to go a roundabout way to get
there, or they would have to fight their way through the front entrance. They ran, but despite
that, the goblins had seen, and they blocked the path. They were not able to get back when they
tried to get to the spot that they had descended, the goblins stopped that option. If they tried to
ascend now, they could get shot or caught by the goblins easier going up the mountain.
They had a better chance to get through the flat area, through the front, than they did
getting back up the mountain, so that is what they did. They ran towards the entrance, and they
were chased by about twenty goblins. They were chased, but they felt that they were losing
ground. They would have to get out of there and then continue running. They might have to
fight. Andy drew his sword, and Liam already had his sword out, but he raised it and ran as fast
as he could to get the momentum to break through the crowd.
Liam knew the others were to his sides, but he was focused on running, and he also had
to pay attention to his footing, for if he did not, he would trip, and then that would not be good.
They ran, then they got to the crowd that they planned to fight through, though they
were only three. They went to their full speed, and when they got to the goblins, Liam slashed,
and they slashed through them fiercely. The goblins saw them, and they were only able to draw
their swords moments before the three plowed through. There was not a thick line, but there
were still many of them, a hundred or so. They were able to cut through, but the goblins
managed to get Andy. They had cut him across his face, and it was deep, but they could not stop.
They kept going, and he was able to continue, but Andy was also fighting well, with the same
determination that he had when they fought the Wincher Wales.
They broke through, and kept running, though the goblins were fast on their backs. They
were then running like crazy through the forest. Now, they had to find a place that they could
get away from, and lose the goblins. Goblins could not see through things, but they could see
well in the dark, they would have to pick a spot well enough that the goblins could not find them
for a while.
They ran and ran, but they only came across trees. The forest did not end. They decided
to turn to the left, but the goblins knew where they were going. They would have to be clever
enough to outwit the goblins. What were the goblins’ weaknesses? Liam did not know of any of
them, at least not one that would help. They might be able to overtake their minds, though they
were strongest in the night time when the sun was hidden.
Andy suddenly took a turn as if he was guided to do so, but Liam did not know where the
guidance would be from. They both followed, though they did not know if he was going to try to
trick them, or if he had seen something they had not. He was running toward a cave, and Liam
thought he saw something go into the cave before they reached it, but he knew not what it was.
They entered the cave, and they could hear the goblins after them, but the floor was
stone, so the goblins could no longer see the tracks. There were many offshoots to the cave, and
they followed Andy through the cave as he received guidance from the invisible source.
Somehow, they did not lose their sight in the dark. He turned with precision, knowing where he
wanted to go before he took the turn. Left, left, right, left again. They took too many turns, but
Liam did not know how to get back out now.
In the cave maze, they finally stopped as they reached a dead end. Whether it was the
end of them, or the end of the chase, Liam did not know. He knew that the goblins boots had
faded, though they received echoes of the sound throughout the time that they were there.
On the side of the chamber, Andy was searching for something, something that was on

He pushed in on the area and he found the thing he had looked for. A small stone trapdoor
opened at the top of the room, and they jumped out of the way, right as a small ladder fell from

Andy began to climb the latter, then Nia, then Liam chose to bring up the rear. The small

smaller than many, but he still had a hard time getting through the space.
They were able to get to the top, but Liam still had a hard time doing so. When they got

crouching. They walked for a while, but this time, they were not hindered by the trees, nor the
mountains.
They emerged into another cave that was about the size of the last one, but that was not

thought that it was the direction of the great tree, though he could not be sure, he had lost his

They took a rest, and stretched their backs from the strain of crouching, though Liam

Andy was able to lead them well through the tunnels, but Liam did not know how much

had not stopped walking for a long time, though Liam did not know what it was.
The ceiling started to rise, and Liam was able to stand up as he walked, and he was
grateful for the opportunity that he would not have to stretch his back, and hopefully he would
not have to crouch any more. Andy thought that they should keep moving, but Liam had
something enter his mind. Perhaps Andy felt it too, but if so, he did not do anything about it.
Liam would do it, and he understood Andy’s drive, but something felt off about his
hurried demeanor. Andy kept going, and then they could see light at the end of the tunnel. They
were there to get the work of the dragon done, and if they were to help the dragon in time, they
would need to be quick. Liam thought it would be easier to get back to the tree now that he
could stand straight, it would also give him an opportunity to stretch his back.
They approached the end of the tunnel and they saw the light that was now blinding, for
they had been in the tunnel for a while now. Time seemed to fly, but it also dragged in the
moment. Laiam was grateful for the sun, for it always lightened his mood, even if he was already
in a good one. Liam felt like he hid a lot of his emotions, but he could not break the habit, no
matter how much he tried.
Liam emerged from the cave after the others, and he saw that they were already there at

Liam was ecstatic to be going to the next region, but he suspected the dragon would give

They were there to help the dragon and to bring peace to the land, as Liam understood

22: A Deathspell
Liam was in the great tree and he was in the dragon's area, and he was talking to it with
Nia and Andy. They were discussing the plans that they were to do, but the dragon needed the
water so he could be healthy and have the proper conversation. They pulled a bottle out of
Andys bag, and handed it to the dragon. It used its talons to take out the cork that was on them,
then gulped it down.
The effects were slow, but the dragon perked up and was able to talk comfortably with
them. They talked about the plans that they had for where they would go next. The dragon
suggested that they go to the plains, for the people there were experiencing the wrath of
something unseen. They were there in terror and grief, and they had no way of defense.
Liam wanted them to do that which they knew was safe, but also that they could totally
accomplish, and Liam did not want them to turn their goal around by having something go
wrong, but he knew that they would find a way to do something. He did not want to cause
further suffering to the people, but he knew that they would need to do many things to help
them. Doing good was always harder than to mess something up, and Liam did not like to cause
anyone suffering in the first place.
They talked more about the things that were important as well as the things on their
mind. They also talked about more important things that they had to be serious about. They
were talking also about things of little importance that was only to pass the time until night fell,
for they were tired, but also needed to get the sleep they needed while not insulting the dragon.
When the dragon was done with things to talk about, he turned around and went to the back of
the room to get something. They did not know what this thing was, but they knew that it was
important.
As the dragon came back from the back of the room, it carried something small within
its talons that they could not see. It approached Liam and held the item up, it was a beautiful
golden necklace with a great sapphire and emerald inside. I want you to return this to the queen of
the sky when you get the chance, if you lose it, you will pay dearly. I found this fallen from the sky one
day as I wandered, though I can not say it is mine.
What does she look like? Nia asked with curiosity. Liam wondered the same thing, and he
did not know how to ask, so Nia had.
Ah, she is most beautiful, more beautiful than any other that I have ever seen, but I am a dragon,
and care not for love, for I am immortal. The comment was expected and shocking at the same
time, they had not known that the dragons were immortal, but how could they fail if they had
time to try everything? Andy’s eyebrows were raised, and Nia’s mouth was slightly open, he
himself was awed by the comment.
Did you not know that we are immortal? Well, if you didn’t, then you know now. But seriously,
you need to return this to her. I have heard that the Oakenash have gone to war, along with the emerald
dragon; I feel the call myself, and perhaps we will join them and help them with my strength.
As a final conclusion, where should we go and what do we do there? Nia asked with a
thoughtful expression.
When they were done talking and they had gotten their conclusion, they were prepared
to accomplish it. They would go to the plains, instead of going to the rock region as they had
planned, but when they heard the news about the people in the plains, they desperately wanted

know.
Liam thought that they would be there in time for the people, but he did not know what
they would do in an unexpected situation. Though Liam was getting better at seeing the ways to
improvise that would work well, he also saw the things that would work best. Trying means that
you do your best, not the bare minimum.
When they got started on their way, they would need food, so the dragon provided them
with a special bread that was cooked in a specific way, so as not to go bad as fast. They were on
the move soon after, and they were prepared, they were also taught how to fight better with their

They were on the road and they were ready and prepared to fight, or if they had to, build
a city. They had many materials, and they had more of the bags filled with materials that they
had gotten from the ice dragon. The Onyx dragon was moving toward the forest, as the three of
them moved toward the plains. They were aware of the things that the dragon was doing

They may not be receiving more help, but they would have each other, so they would

The first day that they walked away, they were sad to leave the great and beautiful as well
as the Oakenash with their cheerful mood. They always wore a smile, as Liam had seen, and they
never seemed to run out of jokes or subjects to talk about. They needed to make their own jokes,
though they would not be as funny as the Oakenash’s jokes.
They were not as sad that they were gone on the second day, but they still missed the

for as long as they did, but now, they had to move on. They were more eager to move on than
they had been, and Andy even seemed to let go of some of his concealed demeanor.
Liam had never seen Andy so open, something must have happened to him that seemed
to change him, though Liam did not know what that could be. He did know that he had changed,
though he could not guess how, no matter how much he knew. Liam wondered if it had to do
with the gem they had gotten from the desert. If it was, Liam wondered how he had figured out
how to use it, perhaps Liam might be able to use it.
Luckily for Liam, the subject of the gem was brought up as they walked to the plains, and
Liam was able to get the answer he wanted. They happily allowed him to put his memories into

surprised at the amount of information that they had put into it, it seemed as if they had put all
of their memory and knowledge into the gem. The gem felt like it was a living thing when he
touched it with his mind to put it in, though he was willing to put all of his memories in too, and
then it could feel like a person.
Andy was able to successfully transfer all of the information into the gem for him, for it
was much easier, and they were not only his memories, but Andy transferred all of the memory
he had gained by taking control of Liam’s mind and making him put the information into the
gem. He was gentle, as much as possible at least, but it still felt weird to have someone in
control of his mind.
Andy was the best one there at manipulating people's minds, and that could be
dangerous, though Liam trusted him, even if he did not like some of his traits.
Eradris, the falcon, had gone to fight with the others, and he thought that somehow he
might be able to change back into a human and give aid as he could, though he knew not if he
would succeed. Liam was not aware of the power that the bird had had as a human, all he knew
was that he had been the most powerful one that was in the land, and possibly the world.
When they were finished, Liam withdrew from the gem, and Andy as well, then they
continued walking. They had made it a good distance during that day, and they had been able to
accomplish much. Liam was satisfied for the day when they stopped to set up the camp. When
they were finished setting up the camp, they ate some of the bread that they had from the

reminded Liam of honey and nuts. Liam did not like nuts often, but the flavor in the bread was a
good match with the bread and the texture of it.
They needed sleep, so they went to bed after they had eaten, and they were full while
they went to sleep. They were still in the mountains, but they thought they would get out into
the plains the next day. The plains were very close to the mountain range that they were in, and
they bordered the mountains closely. They made a fire that night, and they had a new tent, so
they were able to sleep better and protected from the elements better. They had the provisions
that they needed for a while and they were content as they slept.
In the morning, they wanted to get an early start, and everyone was awake, so they
packed up the camp, and got going. They had to avoid more of the small mountains, but they
were not as big as they were when they had been going to get the special water. They had set a
good pace, and they were able to walk a good distance that day. They had a good time on the

they had to traverse among.
Finally, they got out of the mountains, and they were able to see over the large plain that
lay in front of them. They were on a small foothill, and they liked the view that was in front of
them. It was not a lively landscape at the time, but they knew that it had the capability. Andy,
and Nia were both staring off into the distance. Andy was very entranced by the view, but Liam
was too, all of them were. They were very happy as they stared over the plain, and the setting
sun at the end of the day gave the dead grass on the plain a golden glow that had many colors
reflecting off of it. It was like the whole rainbow just landed in their eyes.
They knew that they needed their sleep, so they set up the camp in a way that they could
see out onto the plain as they woke up. They set up their camp again, and this time, they seemed
to be more excited than any of the other times they had gone into a new region. As the sun went
down, they fell asleep with the dusky glow shining through their tent. They fell asleep to that
glow, and they were all able to sleep safely. Luckily, their tent was bigger than they had had
before.
Liam had a dream that night that he thought would be very important and had some
symbolism. The dream was strange and a little brutal, but nothing Liam hadn’t seen before.
Liam was back into the town, and he was staring into the eyes of someone he did not know. He
was not aware though that they would have to break their locked eyes to see the things around them.
Finally, the person and he broke eye contact. He then looked at his surroundings to see what he had been
standing around. He looked at himself to see what he was wearing. He was in Andy’s body. In Liam’s
mind, he thought it was the weirdest thing that he had witnessed. He was there, and he was able to see the
things as Andy saw them.
Liam immediately noticed something that was overpowering, the feeling of some strange hate
festered. It was a fire that grew into something darker. It was a battle that you could not perceive unless
you were there and you were looking very close. Inside, he felt the (). The feeling was as a flaming arrow to
the heart, and Liam felt it. Liam thought the feeling would bring him down, but it was not so, for the
feeling was not his own. He had the feeling that he was prideful, and many other things besides, though
he came out of the dream, and into another.
He was in a field this time, and he walked along, with the only plants being sagebrush and dead
grass. He walked a certain distance, and then he came across a pile of bones sitting in ash. In the ash,
there was the crest of a king, or of some powerful one that was obviously very important. Keeping the
crest in his hand, he walked farther, and he saw, over a hill, many piles of ash, perfectly in their lines.
Something had happened here, and it was no slow thing either.
As Liam ascended out of the dream, he was greeted by a faint glow on the other side of
the tent than when he had fallen asleep. The others were still asleep, but he wanted a breath of
fresh air, so he decided to open the tent. He looked over the plain and saw that the sun was
rising to his left. That is east. Liam thought. It was not a clever thing, it was quite simple actually,
but small things could still wield great power for good or bad. Some small things brought great
changes, though some things did not make a difference.
Fire that was in the sky, the sun that just barely poked its face over the mountain began
to light the clouds that lingered in the sky to an orange and gold that looked like it was shaped
by a god. Some things could change a person forever, and it seemed so good to have the light in
the clouds and he was there to witness it.
He sat at the edge of the drop that was where the foothills started to descend, and he
stared at the clouds, as well as the sun’s rise over the horizon. The horizon was flat, and he could
not see where the plain ended as he gazed over the great golden plain.
The others eventually woke, and Liam was no longer alone, but he did feel small in the
great plain that lay before them. The plain had no variety, nor did it have anything but grass. It
was a massive, dead meadow. Liam now saw the bad in the world, but he did not let it keep him
from seeing the good, for the grass did look golden, and the world would be less without it.
Liam enjoyed the view he had over the land for as long as he could, but alas, he had to
help clean up the camp. When they had cleaned up, they stepped into the new region. They were
excited to be doing so, but Liam had never been so excited in his life. It was a strange feeling
that came from the thought, and he did not know how to explain it.
Liam was glad that he had his friends with him to help, and they were also glad for each
other too. Showing that was hard to see sometimes out of them, but he knew the truth of it.
They walked in the plain for many hours before they encountered small tornadoes. They
were not like the one they had encountered in the mountains, but rather, it was normal, it did
not have a mind, but it was formed solely by nature. They did not know if the tornadoes were as
strong as the other tornado, but they did not want to find out. They avoided the tornadoes as
much as they could, but they were so frequent. They came down and touched the ground enough
that they had to be on guard at all times.
They traveled for times that they did not see the tornadoes for at least an hour, and even
that was a rare thing. They touched down sometimes within a few minutes away from each
other. The cause of the storms though, might be the fire in that region. Above them, they saw
the massive storm that the tornadoes followed. Some of the tornadoes did not follow it though,
which was a strange occurrence. The occurrence defied the rules of weather, but they did not
know if it had happened before either. The massive supercell causing the tornadoes was a giant
thing that echoed through the plains with deafening thunder. The lightning in the storm was so
big that it struck them with the realization that they were possibly the tallest thing on that
plain, and with the lightning, they would most likely be struck.
Liam reminded Andy of this, as well as Nia, but he did not tell them what to do. If they
had a plan, they had not told him.
As they walked, and they saw lightning strike dangerously close, Andy turned to Liam
and asked, “What is the extent of your magic abilities?”
“I can only go as far as making things bigger or smaller. If I went past that, I would faint
or die.” Liam answered, but he himself did not know the limits within himself.
23: To Stop a Storm
They were lucky not to be struck by lightning, but on the second day, they saw that there
were two supercells side by side, which could form massive hail balls that would certainly kill
them if it started falling. The strength of the wind would be strong here, but that meant the hail
would only grow bigger.
They suffered the effects of the wind down below, and they were encountering very high
speed wind that hindered them greatly on their path. Every minute increased the chance that
some powerful tornado would touch down and carry them off, or that the hail would start to fall.
With two of the most powerful storms right next to each other, what could they do, they had
chosen this.
They could be killed in so many ways that they did not want to think about, and the
hindrance would only give the storm more of a chance to break them. Liam did not want to be at
the will of the storm, but he knew that they could not do anything about it. Liam and his friend
were helpless against the great power that the storm had, and it only seemed to grow the farther
they went, if they were even going anywhere at all.
They were going to need to wait for the storm to pass, which was likely not a time
coming soon, but they still needed to get to wherever the plains dragon was, and save the land
from the fire. What was worse, they did not even know where they were going to find those
things. They were at the mercy of the storm to give them what it had, and they could not do
anything about it. They knew that they would be struck by lightning, but what could they do?
Liam had a thought, How do you counter a storm? There was no easy solution, though he
did not believe it was possible to stop a storm, but then something came to his mind that he
briefly considered. What if they could somehow manage to clear the clouds, but after some
thought, he dismissed the idea.
Even if they could use something near them to get the job done, what would have the
power? They could not use anything that would cause worse destruction, but they would be
saving themselves. If they were willing to destroy the land, then, they might be able to pull it off,
but he needed to ask if Andy was fine throwing all of himself into the project.
All they had to do was move the clouds to a position so concentrated that they would
drop their water there. If they could do so, then they would be able to theoretically stop the
storm. With all the thought they had put into it, the supercells started to dissipate and lose their
size. They had thought about it for no reason, and they had not encountered a sure answer
either. At least they were safe, and they did not have to worry about being fried by the lightning.
Something must have kept them from being struck, for they did not believe they could hold out
so long against something happening all around.
They were grateful for the protection they had had though, wherever it had come from,
but they still wanted to know what was protecting them. What could it be besides the dragon in
the region, but how would the dragon know if they were there. Could they be in some sort of
trap?
Liam's mind was racing with all of the ideas, but maybe that was because he had been
walking for two days. They had not been able to go to sleep, and it would probably not be wise to
have stopped moving in the storm, but they were still there, and they were aware of the
lightning that would have surely gotten to them.
The calmer weather was nice to be in, though they hoped that nothing like it would
return. They were able to make significant progress that day, and while they were walking, Andy
wanted to learn more about magic and maybe some different things to do with it. When Liam
started to teach him, he decided he would teach him how to move an item with magic. It would
not be hard, and magic was parallel to if you moved something or did something with your
hands.
Magic could take away from your body’s strength or energy from other things, but when
magic got into the more complicated parts, that is where the mind got involved. Magic would
not be hard for Andy to learn, because his mind was strong, though he did not have the practice.
Andy would be able to learn the magic quickly, but he would have to learn the words, and he
would also need to learn how to counter magic, which Liam knew how to do, but not well.
Liam would be able to teach him a few things now that they were not interrupted by the
storm, but he would still need to teach it quickly. When they had had the time in the valley, they
had been able to only do a little before the tornado came. They had been interrupted, but now,
they would be able to do something, at least for as long as the weather permitted.
While Liam taught Andy the few things that he had known, Nia silently watched, and
she had a smile on her face as she always did. One of the things that Liam liked about her was
that she was open when they talked, and no matter where they traveled, she had a smile on her
face. She always found something to be proud of, or she would have something to laugh at,
which Liam thought was a good trait.
Andy had something that he thought he might be going through, but not something that
was perceivable to Andy. If Liam could help, then maybe he could by teaching him magic, it
could strengthen his mind, and Liam found that when he used magic, he saw deeper focus and
observance in his life.
Maybe Andy would be able to do the same as Liam had, but Liam did not know what it
was that he was facing or thinking about. Then again, Andy might not even see that he had the
expressions that he did, or maybe even the feel around him.
When Andy had gotten the trick down, Liam asked him to say it and use it on something
around him. He took off his pack, and looked at Liam for instruction, waiting to hear the word
he would need to know.
“The word is fleteya. The magic words can be strange, but you will have to get used to the
strange pronunciation if you are to master it.” Liam told Andy, and he could tell that Andy
started to focus right away after hearing the word.
Liam saw the bag ruffle a little as Andy gained his concentration and lifted the bag with
the word. When Andy had gained the concentration needed to lift it, the bag started to float in
the air slowly. Andy did not know how to move the bag in different directions, but he still
managed to make the bag rise with some ease. He set the bag back down in his lap, and as
expected, he asked, “How do you move something from side to side?”
“Well it takes an extra syllable, but to move it in a certain direction is a little confusing,
for all the directions have different names. The word for forward is fase, the word for backwards,

against their minds, but they could also feel that he wanted to use his power. They had to keep
him distracted until the swordsmen could reach him, or they would break his mind. They were
able to keep the fire from using magic, but they could not seem to subdue him. They had fought,
but they needed to find the weakness of the fire. They would need to get into the mind of their
opponent and then they could use him to break his own mind.
They pushed at the mental barriers that were surrounding the mind of the fire. They
were not able to break the spirit of the fire easily, but they were able to find a way to get in. They
could not break his mind though, they could not seem to find anything that would allow them to
subdue the fire inside. Finally, Liam found something that he was able to tell the others about.
He had found a fear of the fire, and he told Andy so they could break it together, to force the fear
in his mind so much that the fire broke.
They broke the fire, but there were still men fumbling about. They were more organized
now, though there were only about ten left to fight. Liam, Nia, and Andy all joined the fight so
they could help eradicate the evil there forever. They were able to do so, though they needed to
still fight the enemies in front of them. For a brief time, all that mattered was that they fought
and won. They were able to fight with a renewed vigor, now that they had broken the fire, but
they had to focus.
Liam was able to see a gap he had been taught to see, and he stabbed there. He was able
to injure the warrior while one of the ones that fought with them cut the head of the soldier off.
Liam didn't have time to look at the fallen warriors, but they had to remember the task at hand.
Liam exchanged a flurry of blows with another warrior, and for a brief moment, they
were face to face, but then Liam used the chance to stab the man through the armpit. In that
area, the armor was weak, but Liam had an advantage by being taught by five masters. Liam
turned to see if there were other soldiers to fight, but all the rest had been taken care of. They
had killed about thirty of the warriors, and they had managed to subdue the mind of the fire.
They were ready for more, but there was no more to be done.
Andy turned to him and said, “Well done, we all helped subdue that thing, and we all got
the experience of a fight.” Liam felt his veins pulsing and his mind was racing with the
excitement of a battle. He looked around and saw the blood, but it did not disturb him as much,
for he had skinned animals before, and he had had the experience with blood.
They were able to exit the cave without losing anything, and they needed to collect the
supplies that were at the fort. They would have more seasonings, as well as a jar of butter with
the fat taken out of it so as not to go bad. The seasonings would make meals more lively. Since
they had no other way of carrying food, or the other supplies, Andy offered to put the supplies in
his magical bag that held the seemingly infinite material. When Liam had helped get all of the
supplies into the bag, they wanted to leave the area, and they would have to be quick, for they
were almost at the end of the morning glow. They needed to get back, just in case there were
other of the warriors.
They got onto the dragon, and Liam was the last to get on, but before he did, he stepped
on something, it was a small box. The box seemed important, so he took it with him, and then he
kept it. He did not dare open it while they flew, so he would not risk losing it, but he desired to
open it quickly. He would open it, but he would have to wait.
They took off, but they were able to fly quickly so they were able to get back to the base
quickly. When they got done with the flight, they were off, and as Liam walked back into the
small cabin he heard the storm, and he opened the box. Inside the box, there were four small
little pins that you could attach to their shirt, or they could attach it to something else that they
needed to.
Liam wondered what the little pins were for, and he decided he would discuss it with his
friends if they had free time. Liam supposes that the dragon would have something to do with
the pins, or something to give them. They would leave as soon as they got the chance, and Liam
was eager to leave and go to the rock region, which was where they planned to go next. They
planned with the dragon for a while, then they were getting ready to leave and the dragon gave
them tips on their fighting as well as helping them go off onto their journey. The dragon also
gave them another mirror to communicate with them.
They left soon after, and the dragon was willing to fly them to the edge of the region, but
not farther than that. They were grateful for the things they had gotten in the plains, and the
things they had learned. They had learned many things up to that point, but they knew that they
would have time to reflect later. They were going to be thrilled with what lay ahead of them, but
they also needed to be prepared too. When they got the mindset that they wanted, they were able
to tell the dragon goodbye, and the dragon only nodded in return. The dragon was there to help
them, but they knew that the dragon also would try to give them advice. They then walked into
their beautiful, wonderful destiny
25: Deal of Disloyalty
As they traveled, they were able to observe as well. All the walking and flying around the
scape of the land had tired them out, now, they were able to slow themselves and focus more
closely on the sight the land had to offer. When they had gotten out of the plains, they had
realized just how fast everything had gone by, but they could not mark the cause of it anymore.
They knew that they had not done it, albeit for a fading reason, though they could not put their
finger on a solid solution. Without reason, they saw no necessity of going so quick, and it was
only then as Liam thought about it, that he realized just how weary he was.
Because of their sedated pace, they were able to hunt, forage, and otherwise gather a
greater amount of food. It would be a pleasant thing to eat something more than just bread, so
that night, after their travel adjourned for the day, they mixed some mushrooms that they had
foraged in a small patch of trees with some simple seasonings that they had acquired from the
dragon’s generosity. They also removed from their packs some ingredients to make a warm soup
before they went bad. The meal was flavorful and tasted better because of the different
ingredients they had not possessed before.
A storm fashioned of large misty pillars boded in the distance, which distance they were
unsure, and whose path was uncertain.
As they slept that night, they got the chance to rest their weary minds of the buzz the
past months had thrown at them. As Liam lay in his blankets, he thought about just how
unusual their rush had been, and how urgent they felt they had to go. He knew where they
desired to go, but he could not recall the urgency. In some of the crazed commotion, before
re-uniting with Andy and Nia, he had seen something that seemed out of place.
He thought about the brief moment he had seen it, and the memory had not come to him
before. Liam remained calm as the thoughts flowed in his mind. It was a large cat, and if looking
close enough, he could see the fangs. It was an unsettling thought, though he was not disturbed
by it.
The thought faded away and others took its place, but none of them reflected the world’s
significance as that one had. The night passed without disturbance to Liam’s sleep, but he did
not want to remain as he had been, unaware. He had realized the mistake that had grown with
them and because of them, though he did not know if it would last through his life.
The rain’s soft patter on the tent's taut leathery surface calmed his tumultuous thoughts.
When he awoke, he realized he had slept in. The others were out around a small fire that
they built to ward off the morning chill. It was not much of a problem though, because they did
not feel the same push that they had to move as they had.
The smell of freshness and earth filled his nose as he woke, and he realized that there
had been a rain storm during the night. It was a fitting opportunity to finally test the efficiency
of their tents outside of the distractions they had had. Liam had loved the smell of petrichor. It
gave him a calmness, a sense of peace right in the world. The greenery seemed to be enhanced
beyond its name, and the sight was balanced.
For the time that he had been asleep, he wondered now what the cat had been, and
where he had seen it before. He remembered, vaguely, the cat’s appearance when he went to
Alabak for his escape, but the menacing thought seemed foreign. The worry soon faded as Nia
offered him a bowl of freshly warmed soup with the wide smile she always carried.
He was not terribly hungry, but he took it to warm his body, and he knew he would need
the energy. He dared not refuse the bowl from her especially. Since they had lost their horses
they had to walk, which was not to Liam’s liking, his legs were weaker than the rest of his body,
he woke up sore every morning. It was not a great soreness, but enough to gain his attention. If
he was going to walk that day, the food would do him good.
The soup was to his delight, not a great meal, simple, comfortable. They had warmed the
meal early in the morning with high spirits and eager minds. Eager to get on the road, not for
urgency, but for the vast desire to observe more of the world and its weather. Weather of the
world was not always in the precipitation or in the thickness of the clouds, not even its disasters,
but it could also be seen in the personality of men’s hearts and minds as well.
As soon as they had finished their hot meal, they folded the tent that had given them
shelter for the night and set off in their strained steps. Walking was not so efficient, but they
could get a fair distance despite the circumstances they were in. They could make it twenty-five
to thirty miles if they were going as fast as they could, though they resolved to walk at a slower
pace than this. They had the chance to absorb the landscape around them with the glistening,
streaming grass waving in the wake of the wind.
The grass was quite long in these parts, though the rain made it sag when the wind
ceased to support it. Liam could gaze at the ever changing landscape and the winds revisions in
the land, though as they pressed forward, the grass became stubby and sharp in the areas it
grew. In some areas it was longer, though it was never exempt of the new trees protruding now,
severing the ever extensive plain.
The trees grew more abundant as the land sank behind them, and the ground began to
slip into an alternate hue of grass losing its former glory.
Slowly, day by day, the trees grew thicker and the grass darker as they went their way. A
feeling of reluctance flowed upon them, grew greater and evermore present as the trees closed
in. They seemed smaller, not foreign anymore in Liam’s mind, though he was unaware of what
he was in the midst of. He needed the urgency he once had on their flight, but it became
increasingly more difficult to recall those times. A sort of tunnel vision was upon him with only
his ability to act and think in the moment. He could then only remember his life before the burst
of lightning. He could not help but wonder, Am I distorted in my perceptions?
He grew sleepy as the forest stretched its twisted limbs lower and closer. He could hear
them, unmistakably creaking or veering in the lack of wind. They seemed alive. Then suddenly,
off to his side, Andy fell to the ground and Nia soon after. Some of the items in their packs
tumbled out as they met the ground. They fell slowly, as if time was snared in slow motion, and
soon, Liam also felt a weariness sweep over him.
His legs would no longer resist gravity’s pull, and his eyes were closing on their own, but
he was determined to at least remain upright. He grasped a twig on a tree to his left, though it
broke under his weight. His legs had given, and his neck was slack, failing to support his head,
hanging; he kneeled with droopy eyes against his will. When he hit the solid ground, it did not
feel so solid, but fluffy and soft, like a bundle of the grass they had seen waving on the plains.
Then his mind and limbs fell almost dormant, and he slipped out of consciousness.
***
His dreams raged on and his mind spun wildly as he twisted and turned as if trouble
pursued him. Though asleep, he was restless; he forgot all that he had once known for the
moment, he could not remember anything. He could only think about a time that he envisioned
inside his mind. He wanted to hide from the appeasing thoughts reigning over his mind, though
he was trapped inside, nowhere to turn to free himself. He could not… Never…
They tormented him to no avail, and he sorrowed and felt a certain guilt that he had not
done enough to keep… keep… what? What had he lost? It felt like a weak thing, but that thing
might be his key to success. Some disloyalty, someone dealt their disloyalty, but he could not
imagine it in the whirl of his thoughts. He felt drained, energy gone, consumed. He hoped it
wasn’t he who had done it.
Suddenly, a great mental gust washed through him like a tremendous waterfall
descending over him, through him. His mind cleared, and he remembered nothing more that
troubled his weary mind. He slept without guilt, though not with peace either.
***
When he woke once more, he felt as if he had laid beneath a mountain of lead
threatening to crush his insignificant weight. His head spun and his entire body ached along
with a boding tension in his neck. He twisted his neck to work out the kink, and several deep
cracks emenated from it as he did.
Sitting up, he looked around. The landscape was not the dank forest he had traveled in
before. He sat in a bright, green, glade with the chittering of small animals around him, and the
sun shining brightly above. It was late afternoon, and he immediately noticed that Andy and Nia
were absent. There were shallow furrows of ground around him that a small, gurgling stream
flowed calmingly within. A bridge hung over the stream, and, leading away from the glade, there
ran a small trail that was well worn and kept meticulously.
At the end of the trail, there sat a small hill with a roof protruding from the steepest side
on the left and a chimney emerging from the top. There was a lush garden off the trail — a fair
patch of land with a crude fence built around it — a stone path leading to it. The roof was
shingled with thick pieces of bark and covered with a wide sheet of dried mud, then another
layer of bark, and topped with dead, dry grass hanging over the rim.
He stood up and forgot his dazed mind and aching body. He was confused at the
prospect of a soul who would live out here on their own, not near a town or another person.
More prominent in his mind was the wonder of why he was placed here, and what moved him.
No explanation reached his mind as he strode toward the small hill-lodge.
Crossing the bridge, Liam came to the path, and his eyes revealed to him a small pond to
his right, roughly thirty feet across, from which the stream flowed. A small dock, with another
ragged path leading to it, extended onto the lake, carried afloat by long logs resting on the water.
The lashings that held the dock together over the water were worn and twisting apart, but they
held firm.
Turning his attention back to the hill, the detail divulged itself deeper once more with a
window becoming clear to his vision in the front of the hill. Long grass hung over it and nothing
was visible inside the small abode.
As Liam approached the house, he could hear a water wheel turning and the sound of
splashing water. The wheel must have been behind the hill, and its slow turning made Liam feel
at home.
Flower beds lined the front of the house with their contents being many. There were
wildflowers, tulips, roses, and many more exceeding Liam’s knowledge. He had never known
what the names of the flowers farmed domestically. He thought Andy might be able to identify
the other flowers, but he brushed the painful thought of his friend away, he did not want it
bothering his emotions if there was someone inside. He didn’t want to show his emotion.
He raised his arm to knock on the door, and when he did, a cramp ran through his arm,
reminding him of how drained he was. Knocking loudly, he pounded against the hard oak door.
No shuffling could be heard, though he waited for several minutes. He studied the curves in the
wood of the door for a time, running his fingers in its grooves, but soon tired of doing so. After
the seemingly drawn out wait, he started to turn around. Before he took his first step, he saw an
old man standing there with a fishing pole in one hand, and a small basket hanging in the other.
“Hello friend!” The old man beamed, “How do you fare, and what brings you here?”
“I… I do not know how I got here…” Liam replied, stammering.
“Oh, that’s common, you probably woke up in that glade there with a mighty headache,
eh?” The man gestured to the sunny glade.
“Yes.” Liam returned, it was all he could think to say.
“Hmm, well, open the door, it’s more cozy in there,” The man said, raising his eyebrows
with a broad smile on his face, “You're just in time to sit and sup with me.”
Liam opened the door for the man, and he walked past into the home with his rod,
nodding a thanks and beckoning with his full hands for Liam to enter as well as he set his pole
and basket on the floor next to the door. They were both inside shortly.
Inside the home, a comfortable fire popped and snapped in the corner of the small room
behind the hearth. The room smelled of earth, but there was also a fragrant smell of potato soup,
a fond smell, one that they had often had back home. Liam soon spotted its source, a small pot
over the fire, hanging on a small wire. The fire sat below a small oven made out of wood, though
coated with clay to protect it. The door closed, it was still a comfortable temperature, though a
little chillier inside. It felt good in contrast to the sun’s heat.
“Well, what do we say to a bit of bread and fresh fruit for an appetizer, yes?” The man
widened his eyes, maintaining his smile and raising his head to look into a cupboard.
“Uh… thank you, that would be nice.” Liam stuttered with a surprised, wondering tone.
There was a silence that prevailed for a while between the two, and Liam did not know if
he should say something to break it. Just when he was about to ask who the man was, the fire
popped loudly, sending a coal bouncing off the hearth. The man chuckled lightly and closed the
cupboard. He took the ceramic bowl he had grabbed and set it on the table along with a wooden
cutting board. Off of a shelf beneath the cupboards, he pulled a loaf of seedy, pre-cut bread and
placed it on the cutting board.
Before Liam could try again, the man pulled on a rope hanging from a beam across the
roof, lifting a trapdoor with rough stairs leading into a small room. He tied a loose knot to a
small root coming out of the wall, which was mostly lined with clay and wood. The trapdoor was
to the left of the entry from where Liam sat, and inside was just a long hall with wooden
supports. The faint sound of water could be heard, and the turning of the waterwheel, which
Liam presumed was an effective way to build a cold storage.
When the man re-emerged from the small cold cellar, he carried a variety of fruit in a
worn sack. Untying the rope, he eased the door gently down, then walked to the table and set
the sack in the bowl. The corners fell and hung over the edges of the bowl, revealing apples,
plums, strawberries, and an assortment of other berries scattered throughout.
Settling in his chair on the other side of the table, the man let out a sigh, and brought his
left leg up on his knees.
“Help yourself,” the man gleaned, sensing Liam’s reluctance.
The man leaned against the table and withdrew a fat strawberry of his own from the
bowl. Liam, however, took a slice of bread from the board and nibbled at it.
After his third bite, he finally spoke, “How did you come to be here? Who are you, and
please, if you know how I got here, tell me,” Liam asked with a begging tone, though not rude.
The man sat a while, and seemingly ignored the question, though he was panning a
blueberry in his hand, considering how to respond, as it looked. He put his arm down on the
table and removed his leg from his knee.
“My name, you might be wondering, is not one I can recall,” The man speculated with a
squint forming under his eyebrows, his tone had grown more stern, and his face was reflecting
his desire to recall the past. Talking slowly, the man continued, “if it is of any significance to
you, I came from a land long ago that was once growing and thriving; it now is a desolate,
ash-laden wasteland. Prosperity ruled there, and I was a lively son of a king, or at least I wanted
to be, I acted so. In consequence, I was banished from the land and promised nothing I could do
would redeem me. I will not tell you the extent of what I did, but I cannot remember either.”
Liam remained quiet as the man finished, pondering on what he had learned. It was a
futile attempt though, for he was interrupted in his thoughts as the fire cracked again, sending
another coal at the hearth. It drew the attention of the man, and he made his way to the small
fire where the soup was now sending large bubbles up from its gut. Liam could smell it when he
flared his nostrils, and it was mouth watering. Removing it from the open pot from the fire, the
man looked for a pad to place the pot on. Upon the mantle above the fire, he pulled a pad and
brought it to the table.
The soup had soothed its violent bubbling, while the man searched in the cupboard for
dishes they both could use. He pulled another bowl out and closed the cupboard. When he
returned to the table in the small room, he dished the bowl to the brim and set it before Liam.
Out of his other hand, he provided a deep spoon for him to use.
“I used to eat this when I was young,” The man paused, then genuinely continued, “it
gave me such a comfort. It is still one of the best meals…”
“Thank you.”
“Always a wonderful opportunity, service, eh? I think I make it the most.” The man said
as if in a matter of fact, nodding from side to side as they ate, seeming to have a song in his
head. Liam ate out of a bowl, while the man slurped up the soup remaining in the pot.
Liam conversed with the man about many more insignificant things, though their
thoughts started turning to what Liam would do tomorrow. They talked about many more things
and excessively ran on many tangents. While in the midst of their conversation, Liam had a
chance to study the man more. His hair was not white, though a dirty blonde combed to the side
loosely with thin silky hair on the sides of his head. His body was not thin, as Liam expected an
old man to be, but it shone with muscle, rippling, and as thick as any man within his prime. His
face was angled but thick and defined. His eyes were a hazel hue with blended blue around the
rim and an understanding depth contained in them.
“You must be longing to catch a little sleep; your eyes are drooping, and your mind is
straying, please, sleep,” the man stated with a voice like the wind, “Please take my bed; your
head will rest softer there, else you wake up with the same headache tomorrow, eh?”
“Ah, I am deeply grateful for your hospitality, but where will you rest?” Liam worried. He
did not want to deal dishonor by ignorance of the grace shown.
“I have things I must attend to, I will figure that out when I finish,” the man said,
gesturing to the dishes on the table and the fruit that still sat there.
“Thank you.”
The man nodded and directed him to a small room dug into the hill that was separate
from the rest of the lodge. Liam thanked him once more, and let the man leave, though still
wondered why the man was so generous.
Lying in the bed, he was quick to notice that the majority of the bed was stiff, though
with a little spring below him. It was a comfortable bed despite the meager supplies available
there. As he lay there, his eyes began to drift closed, but thoughts in his mind prevented him
from sleeping. Many thoughts continued to spin in his mind, though he was not able to bridle
them. They ran rampant and untamed. The ferocious, savage creatures worked almost against
him, reminding him of the thoughts he had when he fainted.
The thoughts were random and various, of strange, nonsensical items, things he had
never seen, and things he did not want to. Bits and memories of his life assimilated into his
mind and out again, though he had known them before. Things that they discussed at the table
also disturbed his quiet rest.
Also in his mind were the thoughts of what Nia and Andy were doing, Where are they?
Liam thought. It was a wonder of what brought him here, and what happened to them was a
complete mystery. He worried with all the thoughts bubbling in his mind, but, alas, he could not
prevent them.
His final thoughts were of the cat that he had seen, ominous fangs. Inside the eyes,
which were completely new, something he had only just recalled, resided a cabal, conspiring
formula. Its purr gave only a greater profoundness to its baby blue eyes.
The thoughts faded away, losing their sense. They waned until they stopped, like the rain
suddenly letting up after hours of torrent leaving only a trickle, and a zephyr in the air. Finally,
his thoughts slipped away, and a dreamless night closed in.
26: A Fine Rest
In the morning, when Liam awoke, he almost forgot that he was in the small lodge, about
everything they had talked about. He soon regained consciousness of his whereabouts, and when
he did, he wanted to talk to the man. Perhaps the man would have an idea of what happened to
Liam before he fell unconscious in the dark forest.
He got up to see if the man was still asleep, or if he was already up and about, working.
He slipped out of his bed to discover whether or not his prepositions were correct. Though he
expected him to be up and about, confused why the man would let him be alone in his home.
Looking about the house, then thinking about what he wanted to do, Liam decided he
would look outside, maybe he was in the garden, or fishing in the pond. He made his way to the
door, to go outside, but when he opened it, the moonlight blinded him. It was just setting over
the mountains over the trees to the west. It was surprisingly bright against the darker night. A
strange contrast, the night darker than it would be in the contrasting brightness of the moon.
Despite the darkness that descended from the sky, the moonlight shone the worn paths
clearly. Stepping out from the threshold, Liam saw the man crouching in the garden, pulling
something from the ground. Liam slowly closed the door, keeping his eyes on what the old man
was doing, eager to examine closer what the man was doing at this time in the garden, or rather,
why this time.
Walking on the small path, Liam tread carefully, not wanting to disturb the humble,
quiet silence. He approached the fence, and stood near the gate, waiting to see if the man would
explain or comment. The man said nothing for a time, until he turned from what he was doing
and faced Liam.
“Good morning,” the man beamed, “you helpin’ or not?”
Liam opened the garden’s gate and stepped into the garden, as he questioned, “Why
right now, the sun is not even risen.”
“Ah,” the man stood and pointed to the east, “See there, even now, the morning glow
penetrates through the clouds. It is best to do it just before sunrise, seeing that that is most
often the coolest time. That means it's prime time to water, and if the ground is damp from
water, it is also the perfect time to get rid of the weeds, knowing that the ground is softer.”
“Well,” Liam spoke after a pause, “what do you need?”
“I think it would be a good time to harvest the carrots,” the man pointed again.
“Alright, let’s do it.”
They both walked to the north side of the garden, farthest from the fence. There were
many crops that Liam recognized, and he wondered about his home, what it would be like right
then if it had not been burned down. He held back a sigh. He had to keep his conscience. The
man crouched down, again trusting Liam to pull them according to his own will.
They worked in the dirt, that had apparently already been watered, and got an
astounding amount of carrots for the area they took up. They pulled weeds after storing the
carrots in a wooden box to be taken into the home and stored. They continued to labor in the
dirt thereafter, pulling weeds that clung to the dirt, trying to take root, and eventually they were
ready to retire from the garden.
They gathered the weeds they had picked and threw them into a large wooden box to be
composted. With the box they had set the carrots in, they lifted it out of the garden and sat it on
the outside of the fence, while they stared at the dawn, the sun about to pierce the horizon with
its rays.
They could see where the sun was about to rise, and they took the time to absorb the
view. The clouds were illuminated with a yellow hue, not only the color, but it seemed to shine
like gold polished in the light. The sun broke the mountain, shattering it with its fiery orange
rays. A sense of thankful satisfaction filled Liam’s heart, a determination to remember his
obligations, remember past convictions, the truth, and hold to it.
When the beauty of dawn passed, they both went to store the carrots, and Liam
wondered if plants would be growing back home. An unusual realization struck him, it was not
yet spring there in the land; where was he? It couldn’t be the time for growing things. A strange
harvest indeed, and something to inquire.
Returning inside and storing the carrots, Liam wondered what kind of world they were
in, if it wasn’t their own, where were they? The man was last to come out of the cold storage,
and as he did, he asked, “How about some fishing, do you know how?”
“No I do not.”
“Well,” the man said, reaching for two fishing poles in the corner, apparently placed
there beforehand, “how about you learn. A wonderful morning to sit and enjoy a little fishing,
no doubt.”
“I suppose,” Liam replied.
The man handed him the other rod, and Liam took it, examining the mechanics within
it. He had not had many ponds or rivers near his home, he had never had the opportunity to fish,
but he was eager to learn. Though his mind was still set on confusion, he at least had something
to occupy himself, or someone.
The man led him to the pond, stopping to look over it. The sun shone through the water,
refracting and changing as a small breeze blew across the top. Liam enjoyed the same view as he
stopped behind the man, waiting to see what he would do.
The man looked at his pole, tugged at some frizzy line, appearing to be made out of plant
fibers. Liam thought if there were fish in the pond, they would see the line, but he did not say
anything because the man might take offense if he questioned him. After pulling on the line for
a while, the man said without turning, “Go get that raft there, untie the rope.”
Liam looked around, seeing the raft to his right, and hopping off the slightly elevated
dock above the ground. The raft was complex for the limited materials, but then Liam thought
of just how much down time the man would have. Untying a small but sturdy rope from a short
post in the ground, Liam looked back at the man who was approaching, seemingly finished with
whatever he was doing before.
“Alright,” the man looked up, “let's shove off.”
Without a word they pushed the raft into the water. Liam felt the same rough rope
binding the logs together. The man looked like a stranger but felt like a friend, a long lost one,
and Liam could not place his finger on why the realization dawned on him at that time.
Once the raft was on the water, the man stepped back and smiled, “Yes, it still floats,
though it looks like a pile of firewood… Well, go get it!”
The man’s voice was not harsh, rather, it rang with a familiarity Liam couldn’t guess.
Liam walked up onto the dock and stepped into the raft. There was an oar inside and small walls
to keep from falling out. Liam paddled back to the man waiting on the shore so he could climb
in, though it took a while for the structure to gain momentum. The man hopped in and made
himself comfortable against the short wall, once again stringing out the line to prepare for his
cast.
Liam also adjusted his body into a position that he would enjoy through the time that
they would be fishing, and prepared his rod as the man had, undoing the string that was raveled
around a spool. It was not as hard as the man had made it look, unraveling the string; maybe his
had a tangle.
“Here,” the man spoke with the voice of a teacher, “first, let some of the string go slack, I
will row to the best spot.”
Liam did as he said almost with habitual speed, and let the string hang slack about five
feet, he did not know how much he would need. “Is this enough?” Liam questioned, holding up
the fibrous line.
“Uhh… for now.”
Liam nodded and tried to wait patiently as the silence closed in between them. Liam
wanted to break the silence, and opened his mouth to ask a question, but he held back. Instead,
he listened to the sound of rowing. The sound calmed his confused mind, and made him feel at
home. Even though he did not fish at home, he would often sit by a small stream west of the
village, and listen.
The man stopped rowing after turning the boat and slowing its drifting. After he had
once again settled, he threw his legs upon the opposite wall of him and crossed them. Grabbing
the pole, the man opened a small box that contained a fair amount of small black and brown
larvae. Hooking a small one, the man held back the pole with the string in his hand… then flung
it forward, releasing the line. He pulled more slack, little by little until a certain point.
Turning toward Liam, the man inquired, “Like the spot?” Without waiting for Liam to
respond, the man set down his pole and helped him do the same as he had. After attaching the
larvae to the hook, the man showed Liam how to cast. His hands were strong as he took the pole
from Liam and cast the five-foot line in the other direction, demonstrating when to stop the arc
of the pole. As he did, the line came loose and unraveled to extend about twenty feet. “I’ll let you
try next time.”
Handing the rod back to Liam and taking up his own, the man replaced his legs to their
former position. He sighed and looked out over the water, like he was remembering something
long past. Seeing the man just sit there, thinking, made Liam more confused; an emotion he
could not describe entering his mind. There was some sort of grief in that.
Relaxing, Liam watched the line sink, and released a sigh of his own, letting confusion
and worry drain out with his breath. He looked over the small ripples waving across the lake,
enjoyed the subtle breeze in his hair, and the ruffle of the leaves in the canopy. He closed his
eyes, letting the pole tell him when it was time. Birds chirped and somewhere farther away, a
fish surfaced with a small splash.
Liam opened his eyes, but the man hadn’t caught anything yet, neither had he. The air
was a comfortable cool as they sat patiently, and the silence let the birdsong ring clearly in the
morning’s calm. They fished until the sun started to rise over the trees near the hill, and Liam
started to wonder if this really was the best spot, but caught himself. He knows more about this
than I.
Staring over the lake once more, the lake was transformed with a golden reflection,
refracting right into Liam’s eyes. He laughed a little and the man glanced over at him. When the
man asked, Liam only said, “Oh… the sun.” The man laughed, but did not turn to see where the
sun was.
Time passed slowly, but not painfully. It passed like waiting for a holiday to come,
excitement and a knowledge that everything would seem a dream after the time had finally
passed. None of them caught anything for a long time, so finally, the man reeled in his line and
set it down to help Liam with his.
“Well, nothin’ today. Turn the knob to reel it in,” the man gestured and nodded to the
line.
Breathing in, a little disappointed that he hadn’t caught anything, Liam followed the
instructions he was given. As he did, a fish must have taken interest in the moving bait and
there was a strong tug on the line.
“Oh. Jerk the rod high!” exclaimed the man excitedly.
Reacting as quickly as he could, Liam pulled the rod high, and pulled the rod closer.
“Pull it in some, then, take up the slack by turning the knob,” the man said, trying to be
as calm as possible, trying not to distract. Reacting the same, he did so, many times. Fumbling
with the line, he lost some of the headway, but eventually, splashes unsettled the water as the
fish drew near to the surface.
The fish struggled left and right, jumping tirelessly. The man then fumbled around, eyes
darting, looking for something to catch the fish in. Spotting a net, the man, with one fluid
motion, took it up, and netted the fish as it jumped out of the water.
“Ooh. Good size, good size.” The man smiled and grabbed the paddle out of the water
that had somehow slipped out. Looking at the paddle, the man cocked his head slightly and
asked Liam to watch. “You have got to knock ‘em so they don’t keep squirmin’.”
With one hard blow, the man struck the fish’s head with the oar, and its wiggling ceased.
It did not bother Liam, but his mind instead was on how the fish would taste, surely he
would not let it go to waste.
“Alright, let’s get back to shore,” the man set the fish on the floor of the raft, and started
the slow rowing process back to shore.
When the bottom of the raft scraped up on shore of the grayish sand, the man hopped to
shore and Liam soon followed. They pulled the raft a little farther up by the dock, but left it
since the water would not be able to lift it from the foundation of the sand. Retrieving the fish
from the raft, as well as the larvae, and the poles.
They secured their poles and walked toward the home, but they did not go into the hill.
Instead they walked around the hill. The man said nothing as he set his pole down by a small
wooden rack with two shelves. He must have made it, because the boards were more ragged and
uneven, the bark still attached.
Upon the rack were the materials used to start a fire, there was a hatchet, a small knife, a
box containing wood shavings, and surprisingly straight sticks. To the left, there was a fire pit
with dark ashes in the bottom, but a cabin-style building ready to burn. Again, as if the man
constructed it of its light pine logs beforehand, preparing and planning his day out. Liam’s day
too; he imagined the rest of the day was similarly framed.
Moving smoothly, the man twisted his body, snatched the pole in Liam’s hand, threw the
fish on the fire rack by the hatchet, then with the same arm he threw the fish with, he gripped
the wood shavings. The man started to turn and crouch to face the waiting pit of coals and
wood, but mid-motion, he stopped to look back at Liam. He motioned with his fingers and his
head for Liam to join him.
As the man settled fully into his crouch, Liam crouched beside him to help. As soon as
Liam had a comfortable position, the man got right back up, leaving the tinder box, but taking
the fishing poles that were still in his hand. As the man left, he yelled over his shoulder, “Have
that roarin’ bright when I get back.”
“Aye,” Liam uttered, too quiet to hear.
Craning his neck to peer under the logs, there was already kindling set upon the first
layer of the cabin, ready, needing only the tinder to catch ablaze. Looking at the wood shavings
in the tinder box, Liam dug his fingers into them, feeling how dry they were, and what kind of
wood they were. When roused, the shavings gave off the undeniable smell of pine. The wood had
an unusual orange hue to them.
Placing the orangish shavings below the kindling, he looked around for something to
start the fire with. Upon the higher shelf, he recognized the bent steel pieces and gray rock that
was flint and steel. Grabbing both with one hand, he expected it to be easy, but then quickly
realized he was not in the right position to strike it efficiently.
Taking a small handful of the shavings out of the tinder box, and a piece of dry bark that
was on the ground. Setting the shavings on the bark, Liam struck with the flint and steel four
times in quick succession before he realized that the shavings had caught on the first strike.
Quickly, so as to please the man, he gently set the piece of bark under the kindling.
When the kindling had caught and the flame was spreading, Liam bent down as he had before
and let his breath out in a steady stream upon the fire. Just then, he heard a sound like
something sliding in the grass. He twisted his head to see if any animal was creeping up on him.
Looking back, toward the fire, his eyes passing over the quite bare ground, something
caught his eye. The man was standing with his hand on a tree and his legs crossed, standing
atop the hill.
Who is this guy, he thought, then a voice said, you don’t have anyone else to trust now, do you?
His logic told him to disobey the voice and run away — someone drilling their eyes into your
back while you’re unaware was a little creepy — but after a moment of silence in his thoughts,
he decided he wanted to know what this man was doing out here… and why. All the while, they
stared at each other without blinking.
Though he would have asked the question in another way, Liam decided to ask it
outright, “Who are you,” he squinted.
He pushed himself off the tree and jumped off the hill, but when he reached Liam, he did
not say he had already told him. Rather, he responded with, “Well, I only remember…
wandering… wandering. It was… ah, nothing.”
It was more information than before, but it was still a meager piece of knowledge. Still,
Liam wondered why he divulged more than last time.
“Anyhow, that fire should be coals by the time we need it, so just leave it now. As for
what’s next? Follow me,” and the man was off.
Liam followed close after, but he did not know where he was going. He stayed in pace
with the man as he led Liam to a small shack that he did not notice before, tucked in the trees. It
was not more than four feet wide, but it extended double the distance back into the trees. On the
crest of the roof, there was a hole and a small platform going into the shack. The man proceeded
to open a small door to the shack, which he had to duck slightly to get under the frame.
Liam did not have to duck; he was shorter than the man, but he could feel his hair touch
